## Visual tests are allowed again (2026-09-12, #924)

The standing "NO VISUAL TESTS" clause that the #904-#923 tasks carried (do not
run `run_examples.sh`, `make test-examples`, a screenshot gate or an example in
a window; hand the visual check to the user) is LIFTED. From #924 on, a queue
task may run the visual example gates and examples itself to verify its own
claims — with explicit timeouts, one run at a time, and still never alongside a
live `watch-tests.sh` run. Every still-open task that carried the clause has it
replaced by a "VISUAL TESTS OK" note; the clause stays verbatim on the closed
tasks as the record of how they were verified.

## How SUMU reads this file

SUMU owns the list between the markers below and rebuilds it on every save.
Anything worth keeping goes above the start marker.

SUMU writes `[queued]` and `[>claude]` / `[>codex]`. The agent writes the
outcome, in the same commit as the work: `[x]` done, `[postponed]`, or
`[failed]` / `[question]` — both stop the queue, with the question after
`QUESTION:` in the line body. Never put a started task back to `[ ]`.

File a new task as `- [ ] the task text`; SUMU mints the `#id`, so never
guess one. Line order is the queue order.

Every task is `{difficulty:4}` (run by Opus 5.5); the other levels are not
in use. A task too big for one turn is split, not marked harder.

<!-- SUMU_QUEUE_START -->
- [x] #96933372 The tests take rougly 26 minutes. We have roughly 10 tasks in the QUEUE.md if each one ends with a test suite run, we take a huge amount of time only on tests. - Figure out if tests can be run faster? More parallel? Are they running in parallel? - Does every QUEUE.md task need to run the whole test suite? What could we do? You may write into the tasks new instructions on when to run tests, or just make them faster or whatever you see fit to fix this issue, or make it better. — DONE: a full run now takes 308 s (5.1 min), down from 27.5 min; same verdicts (829 passed, 0 failed). Every run now prints each stage's time and the 15 slowest cases, which showed four causes, all fixed. (1) run_tests.sh <name> walked all 843 case directories (two forks each) to find the one case; the parallel runner calls it once per case, so a tiny case cost ~5 s. It now looks only at the directories the name can match (unit cases 1219 s -> 131 s). (2) Every case compiled the whole C runtime (1.7 s of a small case's 1.9 s C build). The compiler now keeps the runtime object in ~/.cache/rae/runtime/, keyed by the compile command and the runtime sources (compiler/src/runtime_cache.c); this also makes every rae run faster (version 0.2.93). (3) The wasm smoke gate waited the full 60 s timeout for each of two headless Chrome pages, which print their result after ~1 s; Chrome is now stopped at the result line (150 s -> 32 s). (4) tsan-check ran 25 TSan programs one after another; they now run in parallel, with the runtime built under TSan once (157 s -> 58 s; a RAE_TSAN_FILTER=106_mobile_ui run no longer fails on a missing fixture). Not every task needs the full suite: AGENTS.md 'Which tests a task runs' now says which change needs it (compiler, runtime, lib/core, widely used lib modules, the test runners) and which narrow check is enough otherwise; the new compiler/tools/run_cases.sh runs named cases, or --grep the cases that import a module, in parallel (8 StringMap cases in 1.5 s).

- [x] #63135140 The rae devtools web often now seems to show an agent ran tests in multiple steps and the clock keeps stopping and showing results and clock shows multiple 9.5 second and 6 second runs. It feels fully broken at showing the full 26 minute tests especially during the first 20 tests. It seems to start timing normally after about the first 20 tests? Check what is up with that and please fix it so we get a single timer for an "agent run" of the test suite. - At around 17 minutes it had reached over 900 tests, I saw the Rae devtools test UI reset, and now it shows 27 tests and 9.5 seconds. So something is very off at the end of tests suite too (at least from the perspective of rae devtools test UI. — DONE: three causes, all fixed. (1) The devtools tailer (tools/devtools-web/src/server/testLogTailer.ts) saw a new run only when the log got SHORTER; when the run before was short (an agent's one-case run, ~600 bytes) the full run grew past that size between two polls, the tailer read on from the old offset and missed @@RAE_RUN_START, took the run for an un-instrumented one and ended it at every silence over 6 s: the slow pre-suite checks became a string of 6-10 s runs, and the clock looked normal once the unit cases streamed. It now compares the file's first line (the marker, with its run id) every poll. (2) Another agent's watch-tests.sh emptied the shared log in the middle of a full run (the reset to '27 tests, 9.5 s'; one was a one-case run at 14:02): watch-tests.sh now holds a lock next to the log and a second run WAITS for the first (RAE_TEST_LOCK_WAIT, default 3600 s; a dead or reused pid's lock is cleared), which also enforces AGENTS.md's one-run-at-a-time rule. (3) A devtools restart during a run (the dev runner restarts it on every src/ change) started reading at the end of the log and lost the run: the tailer now attaches to a marked run that has not ended, and a marked run's clock starts at the epoch in its marker. New tools/devtools-web/scripts/testLogTailerCheck.ts reproduces (1) and (3) against the old tailer (5 failed) and passes now; the lock was checked with a stand-in make (waits, gives up after the limit, runs once the first ends, clears dead and reused-pid locks); tsc --noEmit clean. Not run: a full 26-minute suite watched in the devtools.

- [x] #51960491 In the 3 dots menu, add a "Reset" option which works on most examples and upstream games, and does reset on it. Somehow probably needs to be defined by the app itself? Tell me your opinion. — DONE: "Reset" is now an item of the "..." menu in every app that has it (134, 135, 138, 142 via lib/app3d/scenes/editor_panel.raescene; 136, 137, 143 via 136's ui.raescene). No app code and no callbacks: lib/app3d/ModeMenu's updateModeMenuItems, which those apps already call every frame, sees the menu.reset action like the other items and calls HotReload.restartFresh, which sets RAE_FRESH_START=1 and runs the program again in place (Sys.restartProgram: execvp of its own argv, same process id so rae run / rae watch keep following it). Started fresh, readHotReloadState/readHotReloadStateFor answer "", so the app starts from its defaults; nothing is deleted and the window geometry is kept. Runtime C: two one-call shims (setenv, execvp). OPINION on callbacks: not needed. A menu item is data (an action id the app polls each frame, like any input), so an app that wants a faster in-place reset (respawn the hero without reloading assets) reads the same menu.reset action and does it; the generic restart is the default that makes every app reset correctly without writing anything, because a fresh process is the only reset that cannot forget a piece of state. Checked: new case 1156_restart_fresh (run 1 saves state and restarts; the re-executed process reads no state and keeps its argument); a real click on Reset in 142 restarted it in the same process; 136 and 142 menus show the item; cases 1106, 1131, 492, 585, 858, 934 and the c-warnings gate pass. The downstream game uses the same lib code; its scene needs the node: queued in its own QUEUE.md.

- [x] #23474178 {difficulty:4} One GPU timing module, not two. lib/GpuTiming.rae (#528, 289 lines) and lib/gpu/GpuTiming.rae (#871, 430 lines) both declare `type GpuTiming` and do the same job, per-pass GPU timestamps: the old one maps its readback buffer and WAITS for the GPU each frame, and writes profiler counters (gpu.<pass>, gpu.total) for a Perfetto capture; the new one never waits (manager-owned buffers and ReadbackId through gpu/GpuResources and gpu/GpuLifetime, an `available` fallback when the adapter has no timestamp queries) and is what the renderer (lib/RendererPassTiming, RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING) and the water (lib/water/WaterSystem, WaterGpuBridge, WaterPerf) use. Nothing in lib/ or examples/ imports the old one any more; only the hardware check checks/gpuTiming does. Two modules named GpuTiming also make `GpuTiming.x` ambiguous when both are loaded (case 725), and, while type names are program-wide, no program can load both. Do: keep lib/gpu/GpuTiming as the only one; add to it what only the old one has, the profiler counters: an optional step that publishes the last completed sample's per-pass times as Profiler counters under the pass names (a frame or two late is fine for a capture), and the pass names where the caller gives them; move checks/gpuTiming onto it (or fold it into checks/gpuManagerTiming); delete lib/GpuTiming.rae; update docs/water-performance.md and docs/webgpu-readback.md, which name GpuTiming.collect as the blocking capture tool (Context.webgpuMapRead stays as the blocking readback diagnostic). Check: a profiler capture (Profile.maybeAutoStart) of a walker example still shows gpu.<pass> counters; RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1 lines unchanged in 136 and 141; checks/run.sh gpuManagerTiming and the moved gpuTiming check pass on hardware; the suite passes. VISUAL TESTS OK. — DONE: lib/gpu/GpuTiming is the only GPU timing module; lib/GpuTiming.rae and checks/gpuTiming are deleted. It gained what only the old one had: pass names (timedPassNamed; timedPass names a pass pass<index>; recordTimingMark takes the name of the span it starts), the last completed sample (lastNames, lastMicroseconds, kept by collectTiming and collectTimingMarks) and publishTimingCounters(timing, profiler), which writes gpu.<name> per pass and gpu.total into a capture (a frame or a few late; nothing outside a capture). The renderer (RendererPassTiming) names its marks after the graph passes, runs under RAE_PROFILE as well as RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1 (the [gpu passes] log only under the latter), and publishGpuPassCounters(renderer, profiler) publishes them; the walker calls it each frame. WaterPerf names its pass after its label (gpu.fft / gpu.surface). checks/gpuTiming folded into checks/gpuManagerTiming, which now names a pass and checks the published counters; it also requests the device features before the bootstrap (chooseDeviceFeatures), so it times real passes instead of always taking the no-timestamps fallback. Docs updated (water-performance, webgpu-readback, gpu-resource-identity-design, unsafe-boundary, webgpu-c-surface-audit, checks/README). Checked: a RAE_PROFILE capture of 136 has gpu.<pass> counters (340 samples each); RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1 lines unchanged in 136 and 141; checks/run.sh 9 passed (gpuManagerTiming: 5 frames timed, counters gpu.pass0 gpu.heavy gpu.total); RAE_WATER_PERF still logs; gates 136 and 141 pass; full suite 826 passed, 0 failed. VERSION 0.2.90.
- [x] #58125876 What is this? Needs fixing? Duplicate function? : - `makeDir`: lib/ui/WindowGeometry now calls Filesystem.makeDir, which also creates missing parent folders. The prelude's Sys.makeDir makes only one folder, and the window-geometry path is nested. — ANSWER: yes, it was a duplicate, and a trap: lib had two `makeDir(path: view String) ret Bool` with the same name and parameters but different behaviour. Sys.makeDir (the prelude, plain POSIX mkdir) made ONE folder; Filesystem.makeDir (SDL_CreateDirectory, SDL builds only) also made the missing parents. Which one a bare `makeDir` meant depended on what the file opened, and the new ambiguity error (0.2.98) caught WindowGeometry, which opens Filesystem and needs the nested `.rae/apps/<app>` folder; I had only qualified that call. FIXED (compiler 0.2.100): now there is one: Sys.makeDir makes every missing folder on the way, like `mkdir -p`, written in Rae over the existing one-call shim (rae_sys_make_dir, which already counts an existing folder as success); Filesystem.makeDir, its extern and rae_ext_Filesystem_makeDir in runtime_image_sdl3.c are deleted; WindowGeometry and Filesystem.renderPath call it bare. Existing one-level callers (HotReload, 106's AppCache, cases 544, 941, 1036) work unchanged. Checked: a scratch program (nested absolute path, again, relative nested path, empty path false), emit-c of all 106 programs, full suite 848 passed 0 failed, 136's example gate passed; docs/filesystem-and-paths.md updated.
- [postponed] #90744947 {difficulty:4} Measure examples/143_walker_forest (the walker in a huge forest, done in #79861817) on an IDLE MacBook Pro — nothing else rendering: no `rae watch` of another example, no browser tab drawing — with RAE_FRAME_TIMING=1 RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1 RAE_DRS=off, against 136 in the same state. The task asked for under 16 ms. When #79861817 was measured the machine was busy and 136 itself took 18-24 ms, 143 GPU 18.4-20.2 ms (tree G-buffer 2.4-3.0 ms + tree shadows 0.9-1.4 ms; TAA 3.4-4.4 ms and 'antialias (SMAA or FXAA)' 2.3-3.5 ms both run every frame, which looks like double anti-aliasing work worth checking). If it is over 16 ms: check whether TAA and SMAA/FXAA really both run, then shorten the tree levels (examples/143_walker_forest/terrainSystem/WalkerTrees.rae near/mid/far distances) or the forest density. — POSTPONED: the machine was not idle and I did not stop the user's own processes: `rae watch` of 136 (pid 29865) kept its app rendering, and a browser GPU process used 110-120% CPU; same-setup runs then varied 15 to 23 ms, so no idle number was taken. Found meanwhile (no code changed): (1) TAA and SMAA/FXAA do NOT both run: with TAA off (the default; settings.taa false) taaPass returns at once and only the antialias step runs (lib/RendererDeferred.rae:736-768, GbufferPasses.taaPass, GbufferSmaa:353). The 2-4 ms 'taa' line of RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING is a timing artefact: the marks are separate submissions, so a span with no GPU work measures the GPU waiting for the CPU to submit the next pass. RAE_AA=fxaa (instead of SMAA) saved about 1 ms of antialias in one round, nothing clear in the other. (2) Interleaved runs in the same busy state: 136 frame 13.3-13.9 ms (GPU total 14.6-16.2); 143 frame 22-25 ms (GPU total 16.8-18.4), with the tree passes about the same as 136 (G-buffer 2.3-2.6 vs 1.9-2.3 ms, shadows 0.7-1.2 vs 0.8 ms). Switching OFF the forest, or shrinking 143's ground window back to 7x7, did not bring 143's frame down (22-25 ms either way), so shortening the tree levels or the density would not fix it; putting the island back gave 23 and 15.3 ms (noise). To measure: close other rendering apps, then from the repo root run `RAE_FRAME_TIMING=1 RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1 RAE_DRS=off RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS=9000 compiler/bin/rae run examples/143_walker_forest/Main.rae` and the same for 136, two or three times each, alternating.

Needs renderer support that does not exist yet: a textured, alpha-tested (cutout) instanced material in the G-buffer pass and in the shadow pass, and a way to render each species once into a texture (an offscreen pass, colour + alpha, maybe several angles). Then use it as the far level in 136's forest (terrainSystem/WalkerTrees.rae, today the far level is 5 big leaf patches / 28 leaves) and in the big-terrain walker example. — DONE (0.2.59) except shadows and mipmaps (split below). The renderer already had a textured, alpha-cut, camera-facing sprite pass for the G-buffer with no caller (lib/GbufferSprite.rae + sprite_billboard.wgsl, a C-side RGBA texture array); it is now used: it compiles again (a frozen let), the shader takes the instance's mode so toon trees stay toon, and GbufferSprite.addSpriteInstance packs a billboard instance. New lib/app3d/SpriteBake.rae bakes a picture of mesh parts on the CPU (a Rae triangle rasteriser with a depth buffer, orthographic from the side, a fixed light baked in, see-through where no part is; case 1143). lib/app3d/PropField: a level with no parts that faces the camera is a SPRITE level (setPropKindSprite gives a kind its picture layer and height; its props go into one sprite batch, drawn by drawPropFieldSprites; case 1136 checks it). 136 and 143: each tree species' full meshes are baked at startup into one layer (192 x 128, aspect 1.5) and the far level (past 90 m in 136, 60 m in 143) is the billboard instead of 5 big leaf patches. Gates 136 and 143 pass; GPU cost about the same as before (the old far level was already cheap).

Add an alpha-tested shadow caster for sprites (a shadow_sprite.wgsl reading the same sprite array, the quad turned toward the SUN's horizontal direction, discard below the cutoff) and a drawPropFieldSpriteShadows, then let 136's and 143's far level cast shadows within the 120 m shadow reach (today only the near and middle mesh levels do). — DONE (0.2.60): new lib/shadow_sprite.wgsl + lib/ShadowMapsSprite.rae (like ShadowMapsSdf, hooked into ShadowMaps shadowBegin/shadowEnd): billboards are queued from the G-buffer sprite batch (foot, height, picture layer per sprite), drawn depth-only into every cascade as procedural quads standing on +Z and turned to the sun's horizontal right axis (taken from the cascade matrix), the fragment discarding texels below the alpha cutoff of the same sprite array. RendererDeferred.drawShadowSpriteBatchWith, PropField.drawPropFieldSpriteShadows (casts when a kind's sprite level has castsShadow). 136 and 143: the far level now casts shadows (billboards from 90 m in 136 / 60 m in 143 out to the 120 m cascade reach). Checked: with every tree forced to its billboard, a sunlit A/B pair differs on 13.8% of the pixels, all over the forest and none over the lake/sea; gates 136, 137, 143 and the C-warnings gate pass; cases 1136 and 1143 pass.

Build the mip chain on the CPU in Rae (average colour of covered pixels, alpha kept so the cutout coverage holds) and upload every level (the C shim gets a mip level count and a level argument: one call each, no policy); check the shimmer at 140-400 m in a screenshot pair. — DONE (0.2.61): the C shim takes a mip level count (rae_gb_sprite_array_init w, h, layers, mips) and a level per write (rae_gb_sprite_array_write layer, mip, ...): one call each, no policy. lib/app3d/SpriteBake.rae builds the chain in Rae: halveSpritePicture (alpha = the block's average, so the cutout keeps its coverage; colour = the alpha-weighted average of the covered pixels only, so outlines do not darken) and uploadSpritePicture (every level); GbufferSprite.spriteMipCount (192 x 128 -> 8 levels) and a trilinear sampler (createSamplerMipped). 136 and 143 upload the full chain. Case 1143 checks the halving. Screenshot pairs (camera moved 0.3 m sideways, 136 island 100-200 m away, all trees billboards; 136's camera farZ is 200 m so nothing beyond that is drawn): at full resolution (6016 px wide, a tree about as many pixels as its 128-px picture) frame-to-frame change 9.23 -> 8.94 and in-frame speckle 4.15 -> 3.73; at RAE_RENDER_SCALE=0.25 (a tree about 32 px) change 8.43 -> 7.91 and speckle 3.12 -> 2.80, i.e. 6-10% calmer; by eye the difference is small (the pictures are big soft leaf patches and FXAA already softens). Gates 136, 143 and the C-warnings gate pass.

- [x] #47412570 {difficulty:4} [if var / loop var ... in: value bindings get a var form] Decision (2026-10-07): wherever a binding holds its OWN VALUE, `let` has a `var` twin; alias bindings (`mod T =>` / `view T =>`, `loop let x: mod T in`) do NOT (mode words already say it; `var` aliases stay rejected, fixture 614). Add (1) `if var x: T = opt { ... }` (and `} else if var`): the unwrapped payload is a mutable `var` binding, otherwise identical to `if let` (same ownership/move of the payload, same drop); (2) `loop var x: T in list { ... }`: a mutable COPY of each element (document the trap: changing x does not change the list — use `loop let x: mod T in list` for in-place). Parser + sema (the binding is BIND_MUTABLE, not frozen) + formatter (rae format must print them) + docs (docs/let-is-frozen.md table, the binding docs, AGENTS.md binding rule). Then FIX EVERY WORKAROUND the frozen-let migration (commit 0d5b4a0e) introduced: the 40 `if let opened<Name>: T = ... {` + `var <name>: T = own opened<Name>` (or created<Name>) pairs in lib/ and examples/ become a single `if var <name>: T = ... {` — find them with `git show 0d5b4a0e -U0 | grep 'own opened\|own created'`; also scan that commit and the tree for any other place where a `let` was turned into a mutable copy only because no `if var`/`loop var ... in` existed, and convert it; leave pre-existing hand-written `if let opened...` idioms (e.g. lib/RendererPassTiming.rae) converted too when they are the same shape. Fixtures: if var (mutate payload, else-if var, payload ownership with a heap type, frozen-let error still fires for if let), loop var in (mutating the copy leaves the list unchanged), parse errors for `if var x: mod T =>` and `loop var x: view T in`. Bump compiler/VERSION PATCH; full suite + example compile gate (82 examples, RAE_FORMAT=check). DONE (0.1.201): `if var x: T = opt` (and `} else if var`) in the parser and formatter; the payload is a BIND_MUTABLE var with the same move/drop as `if let` (heap payload leak-checked). `loop var x: T in list` already existed (a copy; the parser already rejected reference forms) — now documented and tested. A `var` alias (`if var x: mod T =>`, `loop var x: view T in`) stays a parse error (the existing 'aliases are bind-once' / 'collection reference bindings are aliases' messages). Converted: all 40 migration workarounds plus 5 older same-shape pairs (45 `if let tmp {` + `var x = own tmp` → `if var x`, incl. RendererPassTiming and the three `var opened = own recording` in Gbuffer/Gpu2dCanvasFrame/ShadowMaps), and 3 hand-written copies (Particles `pp` → `if var particle`, Mesh weld `head` → `if var nodeIndex`, WaterSystem `primaryBody` → `if var body`); fixture 775 regains its point (the payload borrowed through mod with no second binding). No `loop let` + var-copy sites existed. Fixtures 1019 (if var / else-if var / heap payload / loop var copy leaves the list), 1020 (var alias parse errors), 1021 (frozen if-let payload still rejected). Docs: let-is-frozen.md (the let/var table, the loop-copy trap), binding-modes-design.md, AGENTS.md. Example gate 82/82 (RAE_FORMAT=check); suite 673/0.
- [x] #85003737 {difficulty:4} ``` Task: Design a server benchmark suite for Rae (HTTP first, then WebSocket / game-server style) Context We're starting to write servers in Rae: web UI backends and game servers. Before we build real ones, we want a small benchmark suite. The plan is to port simple reference servers to Rae and measure them against the same servers in Rust and JavaScript (Node, and possibly Bun). I'm new to the server area, so explain web-server specifics briefly when they matter for a decision. You have room to design this yourself. Follow our usual flow: investigate → write up a design with the forks surfaced → I decide → implement. Don't implement before I approve the design. Reference material (read it, don't treat it as gospel) - hashrocket/websocket-shootout: tiny WebSocket protocol with two messages, `echo` and `broadcast`, plus its Go load tool `websocket-bench`. It dates from 2016, so its Rust and JS servers use outdated libraries. - sharkbench/sharkbench: HTTP + JSON + I/O benchmark, one folder per implementation, runs limited to 1 core via Docker. - the-benchmarker/web-frameworks: mid-size, per-framework config.yaml. - TechEmpower FrameworkBenchmarks: too big to use, but its test-type definitions (plaintext, json, …) are the de facto standard spec. - Load generators: oha / wrk / bombardier for HTTP, websocket-bench for WebSocket. Suggested phasing (adjust if you see a better order) 1. HTTP/1.1 `GET /plaintext` and `GET /json`, TechEmpower-compatible semantics. 2. WebSocket echo + broadcast, compatible with the websocket-shootout protocol. 3. Game-style variant: N clients in a room, server broadcasts state at a fixed tick (e.g. 30 Hz). Measure tick jitter and fan-out cost, not just req/s. TLS, HTTP/2 and HTTP/3 are out of scope for now. The most important part of the design: what lives where Classify every piece of the suite into one of these buckets, and justify it in the design doc: A. Committed to the rae repo Likely candidates: - the Rae server implementations - our own short written spec for each benchmark, so we aren't bound to an external repo's drift - runner and fetch scripts - a pinned manifest of external sources (repo URL + commit SHA) - a correctness checker that validates responses before any timing runs Decide where this lives (e.g. a top-level `bench/` vs under `examples/`), following the repo's existing conventions and module naming rules (folders camelCase, files PascalCase). B. External, fetched not vendored Cloned reference repos and load-generator tools, pulled by a script at pinned SHAs into a gitignored directory. Decide whether we use the external Rust/JS reference servers as-is, or write and commit our own small, modern Rust/JS references (e.g. tokio/hyper or axum, Node `http`/`ws`, uWebSockets.js). Note that the shootout's are outdated. If any external code gets copied into the repo, check its license and keep attribution. C. Generated, never committed Build outputs, raw result files (JSON/CSV), charts and reports, all produced by scripts. Decide whether anything derived is committed (e.g. a dated summary table in docs as a baseline), or whether results stay entirely local or in CI artifacts. Also decide - How runs stay fair: same machine, warmup, multiple runs, core pinning or limits, and which metrics (req/s, p50/p99 latency, RSS memory, and for phase 3 tick jitter). I'm on macOS, so Docker-based CPU limits may not apply; propose what's realistic. - How it's invoked: e.g. a `make bench-servers` target. It must NOT run as part of `make test-examples` or normal tests; only the correctness checker might belong there. - Dependencies: what the user must install (Go for websocket-bench, Rust toolchain, Node, oha, …), and how scripts detect missing ones and fail clearly. Rae-specific - First find out what Rae can do today for networking: sockets, an event loop / non-blocking I/O, threads, FFI to libc or kqueue. A server benchmark is meaningless if we bolt on hacks. - List language or stdlib gaps as separate proposed tickets with difficulty estimates, rather than working around them. Respect the language design rules: no escape hatches, and view/mod can't be stored in structs. That last rule will shape how connection state is held, so address it explicitly. - Check real syntax in the repo before writing any Rae code in the doc. Deliverable for this round A design doc (e.g. docs/server-benchmarks-design.md) containing: - the directory layout with the A/B/C classification above - the per-benchmark spec - the reference-implementation choice - the runner design - the Rae capability gap list - a numbered list of design forks for me to decide Keep it concise. No implementation yet. ``` I left out an actual design so the agent does that part. The only firm rules are the A/B/C buckets, keeping benchmarks out of the normal test runs, and turning missing networking support into tickets rather than hacks. — DONE (design round only, nothing implemented): docs/server-benchmarks-design.md. Findings: Rae has NO sockets or fd event loop today (threads via spawn/Channel, kqueue only inside FileNotify, List(UInt8)/byte-indexed String, Json, nowNs; no SHA-1/base64). Layout: benchmarks/servers/ following benchmarks/list_access (per-language folders, run.sh, gitignored build/, committed results/) with our own specs, Rae servers, own Rust (hyper/tokio-tungstenite) + Node (http/ws) + Bun references with lockfiles, check.sh, fetch.sh + external.lock pinning externals into ~/.cache/rae/servers. Connection state: SocketId value handles + owned Connection values per worker loop (no view/mod in structs). Gap tickets G1-G7 (lib/net sockets + poller, byte buffer, server time, SHA-1/base64, lib/http + lib/webSocket). Awaiting decisions F1-F8 in §8 before any implementation task is queued.

- [x] #25528835 {difficulty:4} [compiler — assigning a new value to a `mod` parameter of an owning aggregate leaks the old one] `func f(into: mod ComponentTable(Transform3D)) { into = createComponentTable(Transform3D) }` compiles to `*into = <new>` with no drop of the old value: every call leaks the old table's lists (found in lib/physicsScenes/PoseBlend.rae copyTransforms; the vehicle gate's memory check caught ~480 buffers/s). Worked around by updating the table in place. Fix: drop the old value before storing through a mod reference (as a local `var` assignment does), with a fixture under RAE_MEM_STATS. — Done (2026-10-06). Narrower and wider than reported: it was not about `mod` parameters (a plain struct, a field, a List and a project-defined generic all dropped correctly through `mod`). It was specific to ComponentTable(T), and it also hit plain local reassignment (`var t: ComponentTable(Int) = ...; t = createComponentTable(Int)` leaked 4 lists per store). Cause: emit_drop_for_value (compiler/src/c_stmt.c), the shared drop used by stores and statement temporaries, released a generic struct instance only when generic_struct_template accepted it. That helper deliberately excludes the container-shaped generics (ComponentTable, ...) from the struct-shape walk, so no drop was emitted, although rae_drop_struct_<instance> exists (the scope-exit drop calls it). Fix: fall back to the type declaration (a generic, non-c_struct user type), keeping the check that the drop helper was synthesised for the instance. Fixture 1006_store_drops_old_aggregate covers stores of a plain struct, a project generic and ComponentTable(Transform3D) through `mod`, and a local ComponentTable(Int) reassignment. It fails without the fix (runner: 400 buffers outstanding) and passes with it. Full suite 657/0; example gate passes for 112, 114, 121 (its hover-leak, growth and leaks checks), 124 and 125. PoseBlend.copyTransforms keeps its in-place update (cheaper than reallocating). Version 0.1.168.

Found in the vehicle examples' pose interpolation; worked around with an entry-by-entry copy (physicsScenes/PoseBlend.copyTransforms). Generate deep copies for generic struct instantiations that carry Lists (or reject the copy in sema with a clear message); add a fixture. — Done (2026-10-06). The C backend (compiler/src/c_backend.c) synthesised deep copies only for non-generic structs (Pass A) and the leaf containers (Pass B), though every copy site calls rae_deep_copy_<instance>. Fix, mirroring the drop side: a copy Pass A' collects each concrete generic struct instance from generic_types that needs a deep copy; its body substitutes the instance's type arguments into the template's fields; the List/StringMap/IntMap helpers those fields need are added. A generic instance nested in a struct (a ComponentTable field) now deep-copies through its helper. It used to fall through to a shallow `dst = src` that shared the lists. Fixture 1007_copy_generic_struct covers a copy from a view, a field-to-field store, a whole struct holding tables, and a program generic with Strings. Every copy is independent of its source and nothing is outstanding at exit. Full suite 658/0; example gate passes for 106, 112, 114, 121, 122, 124 and 125. - [x] #67577252 {difficulty:4} [tests — 999_box3d_ecs_determinism once printed nothing in a full parallel suite run] On 2026-10-06 one full run (watch-tests.sh, 10 parallel jobs) failed 999_box3d_ecs_determinism with an EMPTY actual output (no crash text, no partial lines). It passed alone twice right after, and in the next full run. The fixture runs Box3D's own scheduler threads (workerCount = the pool's) while 10 test jobs compete for the CPU. Find out what produced no output (per-case timeout under load? a crash or deadlock in the threaded step, e.g. oversubscription?): reproduce by running it many times under parallel load (e.g. 20 copies at once), check the runner's timeout handling for this case, and either fix the cause or give the case a worker count/timeout that cannot starve. — Done (2026-10-06). Found a real race, plus a diagnostics gap; the exact empty-output symptom did not reproduce. (1) Not the program: the fixture binary ran 2,000 times (20 copies at once, 100 rounds, under a full CPU hog on every core) without one failure, crash or hang; the runner has no per-case timeout, so a hang would have stalled the suite, not emptied the output. (2) The race was in `rae run`, which the runner calls for every case. 20 concurrent rae runs of 999 plus the other Box3D fixtures failed 1 run in 100 with 'unknown type name rae_String'. Cause: run_compiled_file wrote $TMPDIR/rae_compiled_<pid>.c (pid-unique), but build_c_backend_output copies the runtime sources and headers next to the C file, i.e. into the SHARED $TMPDIR, and the C includes "rae_runtime.h" from its own directory, so concurrent runs rewrote the one header while gcc read it. Fix (compiler/src/main.c): each rae run builds in a private mkdtemp scratch dir (C, binary, runtime copies), removed on every exit path. After: 300 concurrent runs with 0 failures; no scratch dirs left; idle-wakeups.sh and the parallel-safety notes (run_tests_parallel.sh, docs/parallel-tests.md) corrected. (3) Diagnostics: a failing compiled run case now prints the program's exit code, or 'none - the driver stopped before running it', and for an empty output the last raw lines the comparison normally filters (@@ lines included), so a recurrence explains itself. The suite's blank result still has no confirmed cause: a build-race failure prints errors, so this race does not obviously produce a blank, and I could not make one happen. Full suite 658/0. Version 0.1.171.

Find the codegen or lib change that lets a hot byte loop over a List(UInt8) run at C speed while staying safe (hoisted bounds check for a loop whose index range is provably inside the list, or a checked-once view), apply it to lib/text/Base64.rae, lib/crypto/Sha1.rae portableSha1Range and lib/net/ByteBuffer.rae, and re-measure against C. No unchecked accessor exposed to user code (the List design measured in benchmarks/list_access).
- [x] #53803782 {difficulty:4} [fastest wins: bounds-check elimination in counted loops] After the byte-loop task (0.1.244: List.addDefaults + set at a kept index, base64 decode level with C, portable SHA-1 1.09x, ByteBuffer.appendText a memcpy), base64 ENCODE is still 2.3x plain C (905 vs 390 us per MB) and the cost is the per-element checks themselves: the same checked reads/writes hand-written in C take 950 us, and C's char-aliasing rule makes every byte store reload the list's length and data (an unsigned _BitInt(8) element type only won 9%). Teach the codegen to drop or hoist checks it can prove: a counted loop `loop i + k <= end` / `i < n` whose index stays inside [0, list.length) for every access in the body (loop versioning: one range check before the loop, an unchecked fast loop, the checked loop as fallback), keeping the observable behaviour (out-of-range reads still answer the fallback, writes still warn). Re-measure lib/text/Base64.rae and benchmarks/list_access; no unchecked accessor in user code.

(2) `WGPUShaderModuleDescriptor{ .nextInChain = sourceList.data }` assigns a WGPUShaderSourceWGSL* to WGPUChainedStruct* (needs a cast for chained-struct fields); (3) `wgpuQueueSubmitForIndex(..., commands.data)` passes a List(Ptr)'s void** as const WGPUCommandBuffer*; (4) `rae_b3CreateHull(points->data …)` passes rae_Vec3* as const b3Vec3* (box3d glue). Working decisions: docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §9's recommendations (stackless inferred, cooperative, one `spawn` keyword, main stays a thread, Rae-first scheduler) unless a fork in the doc has been changed. — PART LANDED as S3a (0.1.251, docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §12): the scheduler. Spawns that wait only on other tasks (get / Task drop / taskScope) and call only runtime-core C run M:N on the worker pool (one pool with parallelLoop), policy in Rae (lib/core/Scheduler.rae: worker loop, own-queue LIFO + injection + stealing, spin-then-park, help-first waits, take-back-and-run for an unstarted task off the pool, worker count, chunk size), C kernel only for locked queues / park / run / block; audit row 13 done. Fixtures 1073 (fork-join depth 20, chain of 10 000 waits) and 1074 (100 000 tasks), leak-checked, TSan clean. benchmarks/spawn before/after: sort at 4 096 leaves 0.56x -> 4.14x, spawn+get 22 -> 3.2 us. The rest is split into "[spawn S3b: resumable codegen for get/sleep]" and "[spawn S3c: fork-join over shared data, 8 compute threads]".
- [x] #22982382 {difficulty:4} [spawn S3b: resumable codegen for get/sleep] Depends on S3a (landed 0.1.251: the scheduler, docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §12; spawns that only join tasks already run on the pool with help-first waits). docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §4.2 / §8 step 3, the codegen half: emit a frame struct + step function for each may-wait function reachable from a pool spawn (heap frames that never move; the switch-on-state step function with live locals saved/restored at each suspension; waits hoisted to statement level; drops of a suspended frame's locals per state; ret/loops/defer across waits; String-pool marks re-taken on resume, since a task may resume on another worker; loop versioning off in twins; a twin shape the transform cannot handle falls back to a thread). Suspended tasks wake on task completion and on a timer heap for sleep (policy in lib/core/Scheduler.rae over the S3a kernel), so sleeping spawns (sleep, Time.waitUntil) join the pool: may_wait_spawn_on_pool excludes them today. Fixtures: a deep chain of waits that suspends (no stack nesting), a wait in a loop, a borrow across a wait, dropping a suspended task, 100 000 concurrent sleeps; leak-checked, TSan clean; rerun benchmarks/spawn sleeps/held before/after. — DONE (0.1.252, docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §13): compiler/src/c_twin.c emits a frame + step function per may-wait function reachable from a spawn (switch-on-state re-entry, locals saved to never-moving heap slots so borrows survive, waits hoisted, String-pool entries detached/reattached across suspension, loop versioning off, fixed point dropping functions whose transform fails; sleeps reachable only through twins or the spawn stays a thread); scheduler: waiter hand-off in rae_task_complete, timer heap, schedulerDrive for RAE_WORKERS=1, try-lock steals. Fixtures 1075/1076, leak-checked, TSan clean, same output as threads. benchmarks/spawn: 50 000 sleeps 9.3 s/2.1 GB -> 166 ms/23 MB; spawn+get 3.2 -> 1.7 us. Not transformed (help-first or thread instead): generic functions, waits in collection loops/conditions/defer, twin calls with String results or complex arguments.
- [x] #10931037 {difficulty:4} [spawn S3c: fork-join over shared data, 8 compute threads] Depends on S3a (landed 0.1.251). Two findings of the S3a measurement (docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §12): (1) fork-join over data is bound by copying the halves (the 16 M sum stays 0.06x of sequential: one copy costs more than the whole sum), so tasks inside a taskScope need to read data owned outside it through a `view` (§3.1's open point; today a view argument makes the spawn sequential). That is a language rule: write the proposal into the design doc and ask before implementing. (2) The waiting main thread is not a worker, so 7 of 8 threads compute: let a waiting off-pool thread help (or count it in the pool) and measure the near-linear fork-join target on 8 cores with only a leaf-size cut-off. — PART (2) DONE (0.1.253, docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §14.1): a waiting off-pool thread (main) now helps like a worker (depth cap 32), so 8 threads compute; benchmarks/spawn `work` mode (copy-free fork-join): 6.8-7.1x on 8 cores from 64 to 32 768 leaves, 1.0x on one worker. PART (1) DONE (0.1.254, §14.2, approved as recommended): a spawn inside a taskScope lends a `view` of a local declared before the scope / a parameter / a string literal; the lent local is frozen until the scope ends; the scope keeps a task group and waits for its borrowers at its end and before ret/break/continue (instead of forbidding Tasks to escape, so List(Task) works); `mod` and lending outside a taskScope stay errors. benchmarks/spawn sum, 16 M ints, 8 workers: lent fork-join 1.4-1.9 ms vs copying 31-46 ms, = parallelLoop 1.4 ms (memory-bound). Fixtures 1077 (also TSan), 1078.

Either sema should reject `.length` on String (no such field) with a pointer to the byte-length function, or codegen should lower it; found during the taskScope view-lending work (0.1.254). — DONE (0.1.255): sema now reports a member the type does not have (it was not just String: `point.y` on a struct without `y`, and `count.size` on an Int, also went through to cc). - [x] #32040863 {difficulty:4} [spawn S5: blocking externs + fairness] Depends on S3. docs/lightweight-spawn-design.md §4.4-§4.5 / §8 step 5, forks F2 and F3: implement the APPROVED `blocking` keyword (2026-10-08; §4.4: written before `unsafe extern`, valid only on extern declarations, sema rejects it elsewhere, rae format prints it; inside a lightweight task a call to a `blocking` extern runs on a blocking-call thread while the task suspends, outside a task it changes nothing) and mark the stdlib externs that can wait (file I/O; audit lib/ for others) and keep fairness cooperative unless S3's measurements show starvation (then propose back-edge yield checks). Add a masked-tail API to lib/Float4: `Float4.loadPartial(list:, start:, count:)` (lanes past `count` are 0.0) and a store that writes only the real lanes (`storeMasked` with a Mask4 or a count), plus `Mask4.firstLanes(count:)`; lowered for NEON/SSE2/wasm/scalar with the existing bit-exactness rule (docs/float4-design.md, fixture 967 style; also under `--float4-scalar`); never reads or writes past the list end. Document the chunk + masked-tail loop shape in docs/float4-design.md. — DONE (0.1.261, docs/float4-design.md §9): Float4.loadPartial / storePartial / storeMasked / firstLanes (called Float4.firstLanes: a type-qualified Mask4.firstLanes call is not lowered, queued as a bug); one memory-move definition serves NEON/SSE2/wasm/scalar (constant-size lane copies). Fixtures 1085/1086 (also --float4-scalar): scaled add, dot product and masked clamp over lengths 0,1,3,4,5,103 match the scalar loop bit for bit, plus the edges; new compiler/tools/asan-check.sh (make asan, a pre-suite case) runs them under ASan+UBSan with exact-capacity lists and catches a deliberate 4-lane over-read. Measured: masked tail 1-3 ns slower per call on short lists, level on long ones (a form, not a speedup). No lib kernel converted: none has a hand-written tail; the hull kernels pad to 4 as Box3D does, which their bit-exactness relies on.

- [x] #34880336 {difficulty:4} [flaky] tsan-check's 1040_net_ecs_http failed once in a full run at load ~8 (2026-10-10): its line "closed connections' buffers pooled: true" printed false; the same check passed 3 of 3 times alone right after. The pooling result depends on timing (a connection not yet closed when the line is printed?). Make the fixture wait for the condition it reports (bounded), so the output cannot depend on load. — DONE: cause: idle connections time out at whichever second boundary comes first; one falling before `bad`/`closing` opened lets the later connections take every pooled buffer, so the final count across the whole test could be 0. The line now measures pooling on its own probe connection (Connection: close, frames bounded to 100): the pool must grow by its two buffers, which other time-outs can only add to. expected.txt: the line is unchanged, "requests routed" 1050 -> 1051 (the probe). Passed the fixture, 3/3 under TSan, and 10/10 runs with 10 CPU burners.

Decision: Float4/Float8 stay explicit, no width-generic spelling (lane count fixes the bits of reductions and the data layout; Rae has no number generics; a when-selected FloatNative alias if a measured kernel needs it). Not done here: RUNNING the x86-64 builds (no x86 machine, no Rosetta), queued as "[SIMD 2b: Float8 on x86-64]". The README tells callers to write the keyword (`process(x: mod track)`), so the spelled form must write through. Found 2026-10-11 during the `ret x` reference-return fix. Fix the call-site `mod`/`view` argument lowering for primitive (and String) locals to take the variable's address; fixture with Int, Float, Bool, String, a `mod` parameter forwarded on, and a function returning `ret mod Int` bound with `=>` and written through; suite. — DONE (0.2.2): a `mod` number or String argument that names a variable or a field chain rooted at one (`x`, `mod x`, `x.field`, `mod x.field`) is now passed by that place's own address (`&(x)`, or `(rae_Mod_T){ .ptr = &(x) }`) instead of a hoisted copy with a copy-back that only recognised the bare form; `mod x` of a reference binding (a `mod` parameter forwarded on) passes the reference through as the bare form already did. Extending the rule to containing places (a `sema_place_contains` over member chains, tried 2026-10-11 and reverted) rejects two lib sites whose callees do not touch the list: lib/ui/Registry.rae reportPendingComponents (`loop let pending in world.pendingComponents { reportUnknownComponent(world: world, pending: pending) }`, 16 examples import it) and lib/water/WaterReadback.rae ~L113-162 (`cascade` = `waterSystem.surfaceCache.cascades.modAt(i)` live while `waterSubmit(waterSystem: waterSystem, ...)`). DECIDE: (a) the stricter rule, Rust-like, and restructure those two sites to pass only the fields their callees use (e.g. world.scenePolicy + world.sceneDiagnostics), or (b) keep the gap and its README note. If (a): the rule, both lib refactors, a stress case and fixture, drop the README bullet ('It does not yet follow the borrow to a struct that CONTAINS the list'). — DONE (0.2.5), (a) chosen 2026-10-11 ('you can't mod a parent whose member you are holding'): sema_place_contains — passing (spelled or bare `mod`) or assigning a place that contains the borrowed List is a change to it. Five lib sites narrowed or restructured: Registry reportUnknownComponent takes (policy, diagnostics), waterSubmit takes inFlight (5 callers), physics port emitCompoundPairs takes pairKeys, sendContactEnded / sendSensorEnded take their event queue + a view of Physics, both VehicleSpawn despawnCrates/despawnVehicle move the id list out for the loop. Since 2026-10-07 (part of the 0.2.0 milestone) upstream Box3D's C library is Rae's physics engine with an ECS layer, lib/box3d/ecs: the Physics resource on a fixed step, PhysicsSpawn / PhysicsSystems, contact events, PhysicsQueries, addVenueMesh / addVenueHeightField (VenueMesh.rae) for static ground, and CharacterMover (Box3D's samples/mover.cpp loop) driven through the CharacterBody component and moveCharacter. 114 is Z-up like the rest of Rae, and so is lib/box3d/ecs (gravity -9.81 on z): create each character with createCharacterBody (a Z-up mover, as 123's SceneSpawn does; the bare createCharacterMover is the sample's Y-up), and stand Box3D's height field (grid on local x/z, heights on local y) up into the Z-up terrain with addVenueHeightField's transform, as lib/physicsScenes/box3d/VehicleSpawn.rae does for the vehicle course. Move 114 onto it, so the third-person character example is the base other examples and downstream games copy for a character in a physics world — a downstream third-person game that started from 114 is waiting to adopt exactly this pattern rather than invent its own. Wanted: (1) the Physics resource replaces the placeholder, stepped from the existing fixed-step accumulator in updateApp (physicsFixedStep, 1/60 s); netSystem's seam test keeps proving a body moves under a step (physicsOk=true) and the entity recycling it guards still works. (2) The island terrain is static collision through addVenueHeightField (or addVenueMesh if the height field does not fit), sampled from the same terrainHeightAt the renderer uses, so what you walk on is what you see; water stays non-walkable as today. (3) The hero and the crowd move through CharacterMover (CharacterBody throttle / forward / right) instead of stepThirdPersonController + ground snapping; the idle / walk / walkFast (walk at 1.6x) / run clip choice in animationSystem is driven by the mover's actual velocity and grounded state, so a character pushing against something does not run in place at full speed; the orbit camera, switching the controlled character (the roster, rosterControlledEntityId, keys 1 / 2) and the hot-reload state keep working. (4) JUMP: the controlled character jumps on Space OR F (F sits under the WASD hand; Space alone is awkward to reach), read in inputSystem/InputSystem.rae next to WASD and only while keys are allowed (allowKeys), as a press (one jump per key-down, not repeated while held). It sets CharacterBody.jump, which moveCharacter consumes through jumpCharacter (only when onGround, at jumpSpeed 5.0 m/s up the mover's axis); no double jump. Neither key is bound in 114 today (it uses WASD, the arrows, Q/E, Shift, Ctrl, 1/2, PageUp/PageDown). This is the first example to use the mover's jump, so check the jump height and the landing feel on the island and on a cube stack. There is NO jump animation clip: the assets are idle, walk and run Mixamo clips (assets/ATTRIBUTION.md records the Mixamo licence question). While airborne, pose the character from the existing clips (for example hold a run frame with the legs apart, or slow the run clip) and say so in the code; adding a Mixamo jump clip is an asset with the same licence question, so ask first. (114 shows no key help today, so none to update.) (5) DYNAMIC OBJECTS: a few stacks of physics cubes placed on the island (a handful of stacks of different sizes near the spawn), rendered through the existing static/instanced mesh path, which the characters can walk into, push around, topple and jump onto; they settle and sleep. This is the visible proof the example is a physics world, and the pattern a game uses for loose props. (6) Headless-verifiable: RAE_SDL_HEADLESS_MS still renders; add a test hook or fixture that drives a character into a stack for N fixed steps and asserts a cube moved, and one that requests a jump and asserts the character left the ground and landed again, so neither depends on someone playing it. Box3D linking: `make setup` builds it with tools/box3d/build.sh into $RAE_BOX3D (default ~/.cache/rae/box3d/install), and the compiler links lib/libbox3d.a for a program that imports lib/box3d (compiler/src/main.c); check this holds for 114 under `rae run` and the example gate. Read examples 123_physics_playground_c and 125_vehicle_c first: they are the Box3D C-track examples to match. Not wanted here: anything game-specific (event rules, scoring, which props a game has) — only the generic character + ground + loose-props + jump pattern. — DONE 2026-10-11: physicsSystem/PhysicsSystem.rae rewritten on lib/box3d/ecs — it owns the Physics resource and its tables (rigid bodies, colliders, CharacterBody, PhysicsCube), stepped at 60 Hz from updateApp's accumulator (at most 4 steps a frame); the island is one 231 x 231 height field on the 1.5 m render lattice sampled from terrainHeightAt (stood up with a quarter turn about x); the hero and the 5 crowd members are createCharacterBody movers (feet = capsule centre - 1.4 m, the pogo rest); the controlled one gets the intent as throttle (speeds kept: run 4.2, walk 1.7 of the mover's 6 m/s) and controllers are read back from the movers (heading toward the velocity, moving/walking from actual speed, deep water still a wall per axis); clip idle/walk/walkFast/run by actual speed, run slowed to 0.3x while airborne (no jump clip; the hero via CharacterSystem.airborne, a driven crowd member via AnimationState.playbackScale); jump on Space or F as a press; 24 cubes in 4 stacks each on the flattest land within 3 m of its place, drawn in one instanced batch and cast into the shadow map; awake bodies keep the loop rendering. Seam test steps a real Box3D body (physicsOk=true). Checks: RAE_114_PHYSICS_TEST=1 (stacks settle in 100 steps, drift 0.04 m; walking into the tall stack moves a cube 2.6 m; jump peaks 0.79 m and lands), fixture 1101_box3d_character_push_jump, the 114 example gate (caster count 130 -> 154 for the cubes; memory flat over 90 s), suite 761/0. - [x] #92329254 {difficulty:4} [bug — C build error instead of a Rae diagnostic] Using an `opt` value without narrowing it is caught only by the C compiler: `let track: opt Track = maybe()` then `log(track.title)` fails at cc with "no member named 'title' in 'struct rae_opt_rae_Track'" (found 2026-10-12 checking the README's `opt` paragraph, which says a caller cannot forget the empty case: true only because the C does not build). Sema should reject a member access, a method call, an operator or a pass to a non-`opt` parameter on an `opt T` value with a Rae error naming the binding and the fix (`if let x: T = ...` / `if let x: view T => ...`, or `match let`). Check the struct-rep and RaeAny opt representations and `opt view`; negative fixture for each use; suite. — DONE (0.2.6): sema_analyze_expr is now a wrapper that runs the old analysis (sema_analyze_expr_inner) and then sema_check_unnarrowed_opt: a field or a method on an `opt T`, or through an `opt view/mod T` (which resolves as REF(OPT(T))), is a Rae error naming the binding and the narrowing that is legal there — a stored optional `if let present: view T => name` (`mod` for an opt mod), a call's optional value `if let present: T = <that call>`, or `match let`. A method whose receiver may be optional stays legal (toString, or a function of that name with an `opt` first parameter). Passing to a non-opt parameter, operators and plain bindings already had 'optional not unwrapped'. Before, a field reached cc ("no member named 'title' in 'struct rae_opt_rae_Track'"), a method on an opt String too, and a method on an opt Track BUILT and returned 0; an `opt view` field read through a possibly-null pointer. spec 4.1 states the rule. Fixtures 1108_reject_unnarrowed_opt (each use) and 1109_opt_narrowed_uses (each suggested narrowing runs; is none, interpolation, toString need none; leak-clean); 601 narrows with if let instead of reading after an `if t is none { ret }` guard (Rae has no guard narrowing). Every example and benchmark still builds. Suite 768/1 (the 1 was 601, fixed and rechecked alone). Found on the way and queued: #56441898 (copying an opt that owns heap with `=` double-frees).

Found 2026-10-12 while writing fixture 1109 (the copy was left out of it). `=` copies (spec 2.3): deep-copy the payload when `has` (rae_string_copy / rae_deep_copy_<T>), as a plain String or struct `=` from a binding does; check `var` reassignment, a return of a stored optional, and fixture 531_opt_string_ownership, which already does `var w: opt String = plain`. Fixture: opt String and opt struct copies, both bindings read after, leak-clean. — DONE (0.2.7): the deep copy of an optional moved into emit_optional_boxed_expr, the one emitter every optional value goes through: a value that reads a stored `opt T` (a binding, a field, an element) whose payload owns heap is copied when present (rae_string_copy, or rae_deep_copy_<T> for a struct / List); the assignment path, which had its own copy of this, now calls it. `let b: opt T = a` routes such a copy to the emitter (it used to emit a plain struct copy); a local the statement moves (`ret text`, marked moved before emission) is not copied, so a returned local still moves. Broken before: let copies of opt String / opt struct, a field copy, a ret of a stored optional field (all exit 1 after printing); var reassignment and struct-literal fields were already right. Fixture 1110_opt_copy_owns_its_payload (every shape incl. opt List(Int), both sides read after, 41 Strings + 2 buffers freed); 531 still matches; suite 770/0.

Documents/rae.log` still returns the PREVIOUS run's log, so check the launch's own output for 'Launched application' (redirect it to a file; a pipe through tail shows nothing when the timeout kills devicectl) or delete the old log before each run. Converting it moves the grass bindings (bounds uniform at 0) and needs lib/gpu/computeBounds.wgsl first in the composition, which breaks every app-declared grass composition (grassSetComputeShader / WithConstants, including game proto1). Do it with a compatible path: e.g. grassSetComputeShader prepends the bounds file itself, or a deprecation step; then check example 114 visually (grass looks the same) and that its visible-count diagnostics match. — FOUND (2026-10-10): the runtime-prepend idea cannot work: an app's own grass composition is naga-validated at the APP's build, so once lib/grass_compute.wgsl uses the bounds include (computeBounds at binding 0, its own bindings moved to 1..3), a composition without lib/gpu/computeBounds.wgsl fails there, before any runtime step. Known callers: game proto1 passes defaultGrassComputeShader() (fine either way, the lib default gains the file); another app (its GrassSystem.rae) declares ["assets/stadium_grass_surface.wgsl", "lib/grass_compute.wgsl"] (breaks). Nothing implemented. QUESTION: which path? (A) the shader composer learns part dependencies: a .wgsl part may declare `// requires: lib/gpu/computeBounds.wgsl` in its header and shader(files:) puts required parts first, once, so every existing composition keeps building (new composition semantics, small: compiler/src/shader_compose.c; also useful for noise/biome parts); (B) a breaking change: grass compositions must list lib/gpu/computeBounds.wgsl first (the lib default does), the compiler adds a clear error naming the missing part, and the other app gets a queue item in its own repo; (C) leave grass hand-rolled (the 1D helper stays for new kernels). Recommendation: (A) if you want the dependency to stay implicit and old apps untouched; (B) fits the "declared composition" principle (every part listed where it is used) and costs one line in the other app. — DONE (0.1.267, (B) chosen): lib/grass_compute.wgsl reads the blade count from the bounds uniform (its bindings now 1..3, @workgroup_size(computeWorkgroupSize), computeElementInRange guard); the lib default composition lists lib/gpu/computeBounds.wgsl first; GrassCompute dispatches with recordBoundedCompute and adds the workgroup-size override to the app's constants itself. The shader composer reads `// compose-after: <part>` header lines and stops a composition that lacks the part with an error naming it (fixture 1093), instead of naga's "no definition in scope". Example 114: grass.visible 1632 of 65536 before and after; screenshots look the same (differences are animation phase). Fixture 759 now counts 4 parts. The other app got a one-line item in its own QUEUE.md (left uncommitted there).

- [x] #18649782 {difficulty:4} Linux readiness: an epoll backend for lib/net/Poller.rae and an inotify backend for lib/FileNotify.rae, as one-call shims behind the existing Rae APIs (docs/server-benchmarks-design.md decision F8; docs/runtime-c-audit.md row 10). Today both answer unsupported on Linux and FileNotify users fall back to the mtime poll. FileNotify on inotify: one inotify fd per FileNotify (inotify_init1), a watch descriptor per path in the Rae table (inotify_rm_watch on clear), the drain = one non-blocking read; the waker watches the fd (epoll) and sets the same pending mark. Run fixtures 1035/1036/941 on a Linux machine or container. — PARTLY DONE (0.1.268): both backends implemented behind the existing Rae APIs. Poller on epoll = two queues (read, and write nested inside it) so a direction is one epoll_ctl on its own queue and Rae keeps no mask table; shims epollCreate/epollControl/epollWait. FileNotify on inotify = one inotify fd per FileNotify, a watch descriptor per path in the Rae table (inotify_rm_watch on clear), drain = one non-blocking read, the waker watches the fd with epoll (EPOLLET) and sets the same pending mark. Also: the runtime did not compile on Linux at all (pthread_getattr_np / REG_RSP need _GNU_SOURCE): fixed. Verified: compiles warning-free for x86-64 and arm64 Linux with zig cc -std=c11 (shims present, kqueue ones absent); Rae type-checks for linux/ios/freebsd/windows; macOS 1035/1036/941/1040/1080/1083 and the suite 753/0. NOT done: running anything on Linux (no Linux machine, container or VM here): queued as "[Linux: run the epoll / inotify backends]".

- [postponed] #94316601 {difficulty:4} [lib/water] The realistic ocean's waves still visibly SPEED UP while the camera moves (worst with the shift boost), most visible where the waves meet the round island — NOT fixed by 55584e0b. What is known (2026-09-16): (1) it is NOT the clock — instrumented 141's loop (then 119) and measured wall time vs `waterSystem.time` vs FFT cadence with the camera still and auto-moving at 30 m/s (`cameraRig.freePosition.x += v*dt`): 2.90 s wall -> 2.89 s wave in BOTH, FFT updating every frame in both; wave time is `now - startTime` (absolute), the deltaTime clamp does not touch it. (2) The hypothesis fixed in 55584e0b — the radial grid (first ring 1 m, cells ~6.5 % of the radius, 96 segments, growth 1.09) under-sampling the 8 m cascade so the aliased pattern re-phases every metre the centred mesh snaps to the camera (`W.centre` = floor(camera.xy)) — is now band-limited per vertex by mip level (`cascadeLod(cascade, cell)`), and the muddiness is gone, but the user reports the motion artefact unchanged. So the aliasing hypothesis was at most part of it. Remaining candidates, untested: (a) the SNAP itself: the mesh jumps a whole metre while the camera moves continuously, and every vertex's displacement is re-sampled at a position 1 m away — with the radial grid's spokes fixed in world orientation, a snap also rotates nothing but shifts every ring, so the interpolated surface between vertices changes shape per snap (a sub-cell 'wobble' in step with camera motion) — test by snapping to the cascade's TEXEL size or not snapping at all (sample at true camera position; the grid was snapped to stop vertices 'swimming', check whether that reason still holds with world-space lookups); (b) something per-frame that scales with camera MOTION rather than time: the SSR march (#854) or refraction offsets read the previous opaque snapshot (`litCopyTex`) — a one-frame-old scene under a moving camera slides against the surface and reads as the water moving; test with RAE_WATER_SSR=0 and refraction off; (c) TAA/FXAA history under motion; (d) the spray/foam Jacobian cache snapshots (stale-frame counters in the log) advancing per snapshot not per time. Method: the frozen test frame (RAE_OCEAN_TEST_FRAME=1, wave time fixed at 2 s) rendered at RAE_WATER_CAMERA_X=0 and =1 is NOT a usable metric (the view itself moves); instead script a headless auto-move run with RAE_FIXED_DT and diff CONSECUTIVE frames' water region against the same run with the camera still — the moving run should show only the view shift, any extra change is the bug. Parked until the user wants it picked up; the notes above are the hand-over.

- [postponed] #81644075 {difficulty:4} [language design — DESIGN DOC ONLY, decided decides; do not implement] How should Rae's query loop be built? Today it is parser sugar that matches library NAMES as strings (callees forEach/query2..5, the types Query2Match/QueryMatch, fields entityId/index/indexA.., accessors queryViewAt/queryModAt): fragile (the entity->entityId rename had to edit parser.c), shadowable (a user func named query2 gets the sugar), not user-extensible, and it needs the hand-written query2..query5 ladder + 15 queryNViewX accessors. Write docs/query-loop-design.md comparing, with a worked Rae example of each: (a) keep parser sugar; (b) move the lowering into sema, driven by the TYPE ComponentTable(T) instead of names, possibly collapsing the numbered ladder into one `query(...)` whose arity comes from the bindings; (c) an Odin-style iteration protocol — a loop over a procedure call that returns `(values..., ok: Bool)` each step, user-definable, no macros, no allocation (Rae already has multi-value `ret a, b`); (d) a Jai-style `for_expansion` — a user-defined, compile-time-inlined loop expansion that receives the loop BODY as code. For each: what the compiler must know, what user code can define, cost (allocation / inlining), analysability for tools and agents, and how `view`/`mod` aliasing + the no-structural-mutation rule would be enforced. Also survey how C++ (EnTT views + range-for), Rust (Bevy Query + IntoIterator), C# (Unity DOTS SystemAPI.Query source generators; LINQ query syntax) do it. End with a recommendation and open questions.

- [postponed] #20723098 {difficulty:4} [runtime — small-string optimisation inside String: a short string lives in the 32-byte header, no malloc] One String type stays; the runtime decides. rae_String is { data, len, cap, is_owned } = 32 bytes; store strings up to ~23 bytes inline in the header and use the heap only beyond that, invisibly to the program. Why: most strings are short (node ids, component names, labels, tags), and a component holding one today is one malloc per component, scattered in the general heap and outside the SoA table; with SSO such a component is plain bytes in its ComponentTable. This is the ECS/SoA win without a second string type (a `Text(cap)` type was considered and rejected: the programmer must not choose between string types). Scope: runtime_strings_core.c + rae_runtime.h (representation, rae_string_copy/drop/borrow, the temp pool must never register an inline string, rae_ext_rae_str_* must read through one accessor), the C backend's direct field reads of .data/.len (grep emitted C), toJson/equals/hash, and the RAE_MEM_STATS counters (an inline string is not an allocation). Keep is_owned semantics: an inline string owns nothing and drop is a no-op, like a literal today. Measure before/after with examples/121_ui_editor/tools/hover-memory-test.sh (string:TOTAL outstanding at exit, and the 972 boot baseline should fall) and the 10-minute memory test RSS; and a microbenchmark of copy/compare on short vs long strings. Not before the binding-modes refactor (docs/binding-modes-design.md) lands: this changes nothing in the ownership rules, but touching the string representation and the ownership emission at the same time makes leaks impossible to attribute. POSTPONED on purpose: parked until the binding refactor is in and a profile shows per-component String mallocs matter.

(1) Pass `-ffp-contract=off` in EVERY Rae C compile (rae run/build, the run_examples.sh and run_tests.sh gcc lines, wasm_build.sh, the emit-c recipes in docs) and add a fixture that fails if `a * b + c` is contracted to an FMA (compare against a value that differs by the FMA rounding). (2) `countTrailingZeros(this: UInt64) ret Int` (and `Int32`/`UInt32` variants if cheap) as a compiler intrinsic over `__builtin_ctzll` (recommended, design §9 Q2) with a fixture. (3) `tools/box3d-oracle/`: a script that clones github.com/erincatto/box3d at commit 9f998c8 into a cache dir OUTSIDE the repo, builds it scalar (`BOX3D_DISABLE_SIMD`, `-ffp-contract=off`, workerCount 1) with a small C driver, and writes golden traces as hex floats; first golden: the math functions (vector/quat/transform ops, b3Atan2, b3ComputeCosSin over a fixed input set). No C lands in lib/ or third_party/. (4) The List-vs-raw-buffer cost is already measured (benchmarks/list_access, 2026-10-01: `if let`/`modAt` 1.20x checked C, `copyAtDefault`/`set` 1.16x since compiler 0.1.99): record those numbers in the design doc §6, and re-measure only with a 100k-element List of a solver-sized struct (64-128 bytes, like a contact constraint point) if that ratio differs. — DONE: (1) `-ffp-contract=off` on every compile line (rae run/build native cc and emcc, rae init's Makefile, compiler Makefile, run_examples.sh, wasm_build.sh, devtools config/OPT, the hover memory-test scripts, the iOS CMake project, benchmarks, the emit-c recipe in docs/webgpu-bindings-handover.md) plus `#pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF` in rae_runtime.h as the backstop for any build that omits the flag (clang; GCC relies on the flag). Fixture 942_no_fma_contraction (Float and Float64 `a * b + c` with values whose fused result is e*e, not 0): built the old way (no pragma, no flag) it prints false for both — verified; either the flag or the pragma alone makes it pass. (2) Named under the existing family: `trailingZeros(x: UInt64)`, `(x: UInt32)`, `(x: Int32)` in lib/Math.rae beside `trailingZeros(x: Int)`, and all bit intrinsics are now static inline in rae_runtime.h (one rbit+clz, no call). The overloads exposed a sema bug — every call bound the LAST same-arity overload because integer widths share one kind — fixed in resolve_function_overload: an exact integer-type match is preferred, the old first-kind-match is the fallback, so existing calls bind as before. Fixture 943_trailing_zeros_widths (each width, 0, the sign/top bit, Box3D-style set-bit iteration). (3) tools/box3d-oracle/oracle.sh clones Box3D into ~/.cache/rae/box3d-oracle (outside the repo), checks out 9f998c8, builds libbox3d scalar (BOX3D_DISABLE_SIMD, -ffp-contract=off, validation off), compiles drivers/math.c against it and writes goldens/math.golden: 2 483 lines over 49 functions (b3Atan2 edge grid + random, b3ComputeCosSin, b3UnwindAngle, vector, quaternion, transform, 3x3 matrix, segment) as C hex floats, inputs exact multiples of 1/256; reproduced byte for byte on a rerun. No C in lib/ or third_party/. (3) Inventory Rae's gaps against Box3D's step (the b3SolverStage list, graph colouring, b3ParallelFor with per-stage atomic sync, per-worker contexts and arenas, the task-system hook): today `parallelLoop` runs SEQUENTIALLY, `spawn` is one pthread per task with no pool, there are no atomics or barriers, the g_mem_* counters are plain globals, ECS ComponentTables are not thread-safe, the String temp pool is __thread (fine) — docs/concurrency-model.md §5, docs/parallelism-first-plan.md. (4) Write docs/physics-performance-plan.md: which engine features are NEEDED and in what order (a persistent worker pool, `parallelLoop` on it with deterministic sharding, a staged parallel-for with barriers for solver stages, atomics, a Float4 SIMD type) and which are only nice-to-have; the frame-time budget split (physics step, ECS push/pull, transform write-back, rendering N bodies with instanced draws and no per-body draw call); and the determinism rule (identical results for any worker count). (5) Edit the engine tasks queued right after this one to match the plan (keep their lines, change their text; add or drop tasks as the measurements say). Conclusions: threads are NEEDED (even C single-threaded misses 4 ms), Float4 is the margin (with threads alone the port must be within ~1.05x of C), the port design's 1.5x target is too loose for the featured example (recommend 1.2x; 1.5x as the failure line), and ~10k bodies is beyond the 60 Hz budget even in C. Inventory of Box3D's step (11 stage kinds, a few hundred spin barriers per step via per-block CAS on a monotonic sync index, b3ParallelFor, per-worker task contexts merged in worker order, the enqueueTask hook, ~50 atomic call sites) against Rae today; Rae has no function values, which shapes the staged design. Frame budget (physics 4 ms, push 0.2, pull 0.5 from move events, upload 0.3 for 320 KB, one instanced draw per mesh kind — lib/GbufferInstanced.rae exists), the 120 Hz options, and the determinism rule (identical bits for any worker count and for scalar vs Float4). Already safe: String temp pool and RNG (__thread), program args (written before main), file-notify and Spotify state (own locks).
