- [x] #58677780 {difficulty:4} [let is frozen, step 1 of 2: decide-and-measure] Decision (2026-10-07): Rae takes Nim's model — `const` = compile-time value (unchanged), `let` = a FROZEN runtime value (like Rust `let` / Swift struct `let`: no member write, no member/element passed as a `mod` argument, no mutating call through it, at any depth), `var` = mutable. Today `let` only blocks rebinding (verified: `let outer: Outer = ...; outer.inner.value = 5` and `bump(inner: outer.inner)` with `mod` both compile). Do: (1) write the rule into docs (a short docs/let-is-frozen.md: the three keywords, what a frozen let forbids, edges — a `let` Ptr/handle freezes the handle not the pointee, `if let`/`=>` narrowing makes new bindings, a `view` param stays read-only as today) and the AGENTS.md binding rules (fix the 'Prefer let' rule's example so it no longer implies a let can be patched); (2) implement the check in sema as a WARNING ('cannot modify `x.field`: `x` is a `let`; declare it `var`'); (3) count the hits across lib/, examples/, compiler/tests, stress/ and record the numbers per area in this task's note and in the doc. No code migration in this step. DONE: docs/let-is-frozen.md (the three bindings, the forbidden forms, the edges, the counts) and AGENTS.md's binding rule rewritten for a frozen let. The check is in sema (expr_roots_in_let: member/element write, let or a part of it passed to a `mod` param, mutating method call — bound decl's `this: mod`, or for backend-bound generic container methods all same-named methods on the receiver type take `this: mod`; stops at Ptr/Buffer/ref steps). DEVIATION: the warning is OPT-IN (RAE_FROZEN_LET=warn), not default — a default warning printed ~770 lib sites into every build and broke the expected output of 130 test cases; compiler/tools/test-frozen-let.sh (in the suite) checks it. - [x] #61963793 {difficulty:4} Sema accepts `Math.log(x: 3.0)` though lib/Math has no `log` (its natural log is `naturalLog`): the module-qualified call silently resolves to the global logging builtin `log`, the `x:` label is not checked against it, and its void result is accepted inside `let value: Float = 2.0 * Math.log(x: 3.0)` - the build only fails in clang ("invalid operands to binary expression ('double' and 'void')", emitted as `2.0 * rae_ext_rae_log_write(rae_ext_rae_str((3.0)), 1)`). Repro: a Main.rae with `import Math` and that line; `rae run --target compiled`. Fix both holes with a compiler test case each: (1) `Module.name(...)` resolves ONLY in that module (an unknown name there is a sema error naming the module, e.g. "lib/Math has no function `log`; did you mean `naturalLog`?"), never falling back to a global/builtin; (2) a void call used as a value (in an expression, a `let`, an argument) is a sema error. Found by an app whose Box-Muller height roll wrote Math.log for the natural log.
- [x] #73210855 {difficulty:4} [render-perf-1: DRS cap lift] {difficulty:4} lib/app3d/RenderScale.rae: the dynamic-resolution controller's 30 fps bottom rung can never lift. When the scale is at minScale and frames still miss, it sets capFps = 30; to restore 60 it needs 180 consecutive frames with dt <= overBudgetSec (1.25/60 = 20.8 ms), but while capped capFrameSleep pads every frame to 33 ms, so every capped frame counts as missed and goodFrames never reaches 180: an app that dipped once stays at 30 fps until restarted (seen on an iPhone 15 Pro: 59, then 55 as the scale stepped down, then 30 for good). Fix: judge a frame by its WORK time (start of frame to just before the cap's padding sleep), not by the padded dt — e.g. capFrameSleep records the frame's work seconds on the controller and updateRenderScale uses them while capped. Also: the thermal 'critical' state forces capFps 30, and lifting it should depend on the thermal state falling again, not only on frame times. Test first (a compiler test case or lib test): a controller driven to the cap by missed frames, then fed 180+ frames whose work time is 10 ms but whose dt is 33 ms (padded), must restore capFps 60; a controller fed genuinely slow work must stay capped. Keep the existing behaviour otherwise (drop after 4 misses, 30-frame cooldown, restore one rung after ~3 s good). EXAMPLES: verify on 114_walker_character (the only example with the dynamic-resolution controller): force misses until it caps at 30 (e.g. a heavy setting or a slowed GPU), then let it run light and see the cap lift (log render.capFps). RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the controller is already Rae and thermalState is already a one-call shim (runtime_platform_apple.c); keep every decision in lib/app3d/RenderScale.rae.
- [x] #31201268 [render-perf-2: two frames in flight] {difficulty:4} The native runtime waits for the GPU at the end of every frame: rae_g2d_present ends with rae_wgpu_poll_frame_end() -> wgpuDevicePoll(device, wait=true) (compiler/runtime/runtime_gpu2d_frame.c, runtime_webgpu.c), so the CPU and GPU never overlap and frame time is CPU + GPU; on Fifo, as soon as that passes 16.7 ms the rate halves to 30. Allow two frames in flight: after submitting frame N, wait only until frame N-1's work is done (wgpuQueueOnSubmittedWorkDone futures, or a non-blocking poll plus a wait on the older frame), never on the frame just submitted. Keep what the blocking poll was protecting: a hidden/occluded window (no drawable, no vsync) must not pile up unpresented frames (the memory test that once reached 2.1 GB in 90 s hidden), and per-frame resources written by the CPU (uniform buffers, instance records, palettes) must not be overwritten while the GPU of the previous frame still reads them (double-buffer them or rely on queue ordering of writeBuffer). Verify: the full test suite (watch-tests), the 3D example gates, a memory run with the window hidden staying flat, and a frame-time comparison before/after on the walker example (CPU and GPU time printed apart). EXAMPLES: verify on 114_walker_character, 112_metaballs_deferred, 111_metaballs_forward (the forward path shares the present code), 118_water_lake and 119_ocean_fft; 119 reads water heights back from the GPU (water/WaterReadback) - with two frames in flight that readback must still map the frame it asked for, not a newer or half-written one. Frame-time before/after for each. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here: the end-of-frame blocking poll inside rae_g2d_present (runtime_gpu2d_frame.c) and all frame/submission bookkeeping (which frame waits on which submission, the bound of two, the hidden-window case, which readbacks need which frame) become Rae; C keeps only one-call shims: submit returning the index (wgpuQueueSubmitForIndex), poll until an index (wgpuDevicePoll with it), and the completion callback registration (rae_wgpu_watch_submission / rae_wgpu_submission_done in runtime_webgpu.c - the callback itself must stay C, as Rae cannot yet hand C a callback pointer).
- [x] #93852273 {difficulty:4} [compiler bug — a narrowed optional FIXED-WIDTH integer is never dereferenced] Found in #31201268. `type Holder { value: opt UInt64 }` + `if let present: view UInt64 => holder.value { let copy: UInt64 = present }` emits C `uint64_t copy = present;` where `present` is a `const uint64_t*` (clang: incompatible pointer to integer conversion); passing `present` to an extern's by-value `UInt64` parameter fails the same way. A fixed-width integer reference is a plain pointer (no `rae_View_*` wrapper), and the narrowed binding's uses skip the deref the wrapped types get. Fix every use of such a binding (copy, argument, arithmetic, comparison, interpolation) for UInt64/UInt32/Int32/UInt8 etc., add a fixture covering them, then let lib/gpu/FramePacer.rae use `previousEnd: opt UInt64` instead of its hasPreviousEnd Bool.
- [x] #53696563 {difficulty:4} [compiler bug — a plain value passed to an `opt copy` number parameter is not wrapped into an optional] Found in #93852273. `func narrow(maybe: opt copy Int) ret Int { ... }` called as `narrow(maybe: 4)` (or with an `Int` / `UInt64` local) emits C that passes the bare `int64_t` where the parameter is a `rae_opt_int64_t`: clang "passing 'int64_t' to parameter of incompatible type 'rae_opt_int64_t'". Passing an `opt Int` local or `none` works; a binding does wrap (`let x: opt Int = 4`). Wrap a non-optional argument into the parameter's optional (`(rae_opt_T){ .has = 1, .value = ... }`) for every value-optional parameter mode (`opt copy` / `opt own`) and payload (numbers, fixed-width ints, Bool, String, structs), and add a fixture passing a literal, a local, a field and a call result.
- [x] #61453872 {difficulty:4} [compiler bug — narrowing an `opt copy T` parameter inside a GENERIC function miscompiles] Found in #53696563. `func describe(T: type, maybe: opt copy T) ret String { if let present: view T => maybe { ret "some {present}" } ret "none" }` called as `describe(Int, maybe: 3)`: the call site now wraps the 3 correctly, but the specialisation's body emits `int64_t present = &maybe;` (the struct-rep narrowing, `maybe.has ? &maybe.value : NULL`, is not used once T is substituted to a number) and clang rejects it. Make `if let x: view T =>` / `mod T =>` of an optional parameter or local narrow correctly in every specialisation (T = Int, a fixed-width int, Bool, String, a struct), with a fixture.
- [x] #14781103 {difficulty:4} [compiler bug — integer literals are not range-checked against the type they land in] `let big: UInt8 = 300` compiles and prints 44, `let negative: UInt64 = -1` compiles and prints 18446744073709551615 (checked 2026-10-13 on 0.2.13). A bare literal correctly takes its context's type (binding, argument, arithmetic operand — Rust/Swift/Go/Zig/C# do the same), but one that does not FIT that type must be a sema error naming the value, the type and its range ("300 does not fit UInt8 (0 to 255)"), never a silent wrap. Cover every fixed-width type (Int8..Int64, UInt8..UInt64), negative literals into unsigned types, the edges (255 into UInt8 fine, 256 not; Int64 min/max), and the positions: let/var, assignment, argument, return, struct/list literal field, operand of arithmetic with a typed side. A fixture for each class of rejection plus one accepting the edge values. Must land before the redundant-literal-cast task below.
- [x] #75768925 {difficulty:4} [language — a numeric literal cast to the type it already gets is an error; sweep the tree] Depends on the literal range-check task above. A bare literal already takes its context's type in Rae (`var frameEnd: UInt64 = 0`, `takesWide(count: 0)`, `bytes.add(value: 0)` on a List(UInt8) all work), so `0 as UInt64` there is noise — 257 `<number> as <IntType/FloatType>` spellings in lib/, examples/, benchmarks/ and compiler/tests (e.g. lib/gpu/FramePacer.rae `var frameEnd: UInt64 = 0 as UInt64`). Decision 2026-10-13: make it a sema ERROR, not a warning (same one-spelling principle as `-1` over `0 - 1`; warnings pile up): when a numeric literal is cast with `as T` and T is exactly the type the literal would take from its context, reject it with "the literal already is a T here; drop `as T`". A cast that converts (`count as Float`), or a literal cast where no context gives it a type, stays legal. Land the rule and the sweep in ONE commit (breaking: bump PATCH): remove every redundant cast the new error reports, keep the ones it does not, and check the generated C is unchanged where it should be (emit-c every example, the benchmarks compile check, the full suite). Add the rule to AGENTS.md (Coding style: next to the `-1` rule) and spec/rae.md (literals). Fixtures: one rejecting the redundant forms (binding, argument, List element, arithmetic operand), one accepting the converting and context-free forms.
- [x] #30968541 [render-perf-3: render-scale ceiling] {difficulty:4} The deferred renderer draws the 3D scene at the drawable's native size (an iPhone 15 Pro: 2556x1179, about 3 MP and ~34 bytes per pixel of render targets), and only dynamic resolution ever lowers it. Add an app-set render-resolution ceiling: a maximum scene size in pixels (e.g. 1600x720 for phones; 1.0 of native on desktop), from which the scene scale is derived per window size (keeping aspect), with the dynamic controller working inside that ceiling. The UI, the composite's final output and the present stay at native size. Make the composite upscale with a good filter (at least bilinear; consider a sharpening/edge-aware upscale). Expose it in lib/app3d (a device-class default: phones vs desktop, via Device or Target, never a compile-time OS check in app code). Test: the scale derived for 2556x1179 with a 1600x720 ceiling is about 0.61, 1280x800 desktop stays 1.0, a rotation re-derives it. Verify on the walker example with a forced ceiling (screenshot). EXAMPLES: show it on 114_walker_character, 112_metaballs_deferred (ray-marched SDF metaballs: per-pixel cost, the biggest gain) and 119_ocean_fft (UI and text stay native-sharp; world softer at the ceiling) - headless screenshots at a forced phone ceiling and frame times. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the render scale is policy in C today (runtime_gpu3d_gbuffer.c: the RAE_RENDER_SCALE env read, the 0.25-1 clamp, the scaled target sizes rae_gb_offscreen_w/h): move it to Rae (scale, ceiling and target sizes computed in lib/; C at most reports the surface size and creates textures of the sizes it is given).
- [x] #59120817 [render-perf-4: AA before the upscale] {difficulty:4} The default anti-aliasing is FXAA inside deferred_composite.wgsl, which runs AFTER the bilinear upscale from the scene scale to native — where FXAA does little (edges are already smeared) — and calls shade() 5-9 times per native pixel. Run the AA at the scene resolution, before the upscale: a separate pass (or the end of the lighting/TAA stage) that outputs an anti-aliased scene-resolution image, then the composite only tone-maps/grades/upscales. Keep the pass list and the render graph consistent (lib/RendererDeferredGraph.rae). Verify with headless screenshots at scale 0.6 of a thin-edge scene (walker example: grass, character outline) that edges are smoother than today, and that the frame cost drops (FXAA no longer at native). EXAMPLES: compare edges before/after on 114_walker_character (grass, character), 112_metaballs_deferred (curved metaball silhouettes) and 119_ocean_fft (the horizon and wave crests) at scale 0.6. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the AA pass and the composite stay in Rae lib + WGSL (lib/GbufferPasses.rae, the render graph); no C.
- [x] #55611985 [render-perf-5: cut wasted passes] {difficulty:4} Work the deferred renderer does every frame for nothing: (1) TransparentForward copies the whole lit target into litCopy every frame even with zero transparent draws — skip the copy (and the pass) when nothing transparent is queued; (2) the depth pyramid (GbufferPasses depthPyramid, ~11 mips, one render pass per mip) is built every frame but no pass samples it (lighting and SSAO bind groups do not include it) — build it only when a consumer asks; (3) the two TAA history targets are allocated when TAA is off — allocate them on enabling TAA; (4) present is a full-screen CopyTextureToTexture into the drawable — render the composite/UI straight into the surface texture if wgpu allows (the surface usage RenderAttachment), else keep the copy but say why; (5) every pass is its own command encoder and queue submit (~10 per frame) — record the frame into as few encoders/submits as the dependencies allow. Measure GPU time per pass before/after (the profiler's GPU counters) on the walker example and keep its gate green. EXAMPLES: 114_walker_character and 112_metaballs_deferred have no transparents, so the lit copy must disappear there; 118_water_lake and 119_ocean_fft draw water in the transparent pass and READ that copy for refraction, so there it must stay and the water must look the same (headless screenshots before/after). GPU time per pass on all four. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the present's extra copy and the surface acquisition are decided in C (runtime_gpu2d_frame.c rae_g2d_present): the decisions (render straight into the surface or copy, when) move to Rae; the copy-skip and pyramid/history gating are Rae (TransparentForward.rae, GbufferPasses.rae, GbufferTargets.rae). DONE (0.2.22): (1) litCopy skipped with no transparents, (2) pyramid only after requestDepthPyramid (110 asks), (3) TAA history made when TAA is on. (4) copy kept, why in docs/webgpu-3d-renderer.md §4 + runtime-c-audit row 16; split to 'render-perf-11b: composite and UI straight into the drawable'. (5) split to 'render-perf-5b: fewer encoders and submits per frame'.
- [x] #88526033 {difficulty:4} [render-perf-5b: fewer encoders and submits per frame] Split from render-perf-5 (which landed the litCopy skip, the on-request depth pyramid and the TAA history allocated on enabling TAA). Every deferred pass still creates its own command encoder and submits it (gbuffer via its manager Recording + trackSubmission, shadow/transparent/fullscreen passes via gbSubmit, the 2D canvas via its Recording, the C present copy): about 10-14 submits per frame. Record the frame into as few encoders/submits as the dependencies allow. Check first: (1) every queue.writeBuffer a pass does runs BEFORE every command buffer of the same submit, so a uniform/storage buffer written by two passes of one frame (shadow cascades, the draws buffer offsets, the fullscreen uniforms) must not be shared once merged; (2) each pass's TRACKED submission (inFlight lists, retire-after-GPU) must still pin what it used; (3) RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING marks are their own submissions between passes, so per-pass timing needs timestamp writes inside the merged encoder instead. Measure CPU and GPU frame time (RAE_FRAME_TIMING=1, RAE_GPU_PASS_TIMING=1) before/after on 114_walker_character, 112_metaballs_deferred, 118_water_lake, 119_ocean_fft; keep their gates green. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md): Rae lib only; no new runtime C. DONE (0.2.23): the post passes (ssao, pyramid, lighting, transparent copy/draw, underwater, taa, antialias, composite) record into one open recording on DeferredTargets.post, submitted after lighting, after the composite, at snapshotOpaque and at present: usually 2 submits instead of 5-6; timing marks are recorded into it. Headless frame ms 114 5.3->5.2, 118 3.4->3.3, 119 6.3->6.25, 112 8.5->8.5. Shadow and G-buffer keep their own submits on purpose: merging the shadow made 114 5.5 / 112 9.0 ms (the GPU starts later); see docs/webgpu-3d-renderer.md §4.

Run it at half resolution from a half-resolution depth (min or checkerboard downsample), with a 4x4 interleaved direction pattern, a separable bilateral blur at half resolution, and a depth-aware upsample in the lighting pass. Here the new half-resolution SSAO is Rae lib + WGSL in the deferred renderer (lib/GbufferPasses.rae, deferred_ao.wgsl), not the older C SSAO of the forward path (runtime_gpu3d_ssao.c). DONE (0.2.24): lib/GbufferAo.rae — checkerboard half depth (+ kept index), half-size SSAO with a 4x4 Bayer rotation, separable 5-tap depth-aware blur, 4-tap depth-aware upsample in lighting. Headless 2560x1600 frame: 114 5.2->4.3 ms, 112 8.7->7.3 ms; contact regions within 0.1% brightness, AO smoother. - [x] #59486255 [render-perf-7: shadow bias and taps] {difficulty:4} Shadows detach from contact points (peter-panning: a character's feet float over their shadow). Today the shadow pass culls FRONT faces with depthBias 2 and depthBiasSlopeScale 2.5 (lib/ShadowMaps.rae), and lighting adds a 1.5-texel normal offset (deferred_light.wgsl), all growing with the cascade's texel size; the filter is PCSS-style (8-tap blocker search + 12-tap Poisson PCF, ~20 taps per pixel). Change to: the material's own (back-face) culling in the shadow pass, a small constant bias and a moderate slope bias, no normal offset (or a much smaller one), and optionally a receiver-plane depth bias in the PCF (compare each tap against a depth extrapolated along the receiving surface's plane with dpdx/dpdy) so contacts stay attached; and cheaper filtering: a 4-tap (or one jittered hardware-compare tap per pixel when TAA is on) PCF instead of ~20 taps. Check against shadow acne on slopes and at grazing light, and against feet on the ground in every cascade (walker example headless screenshots, close and far). EXAMPLES: feet on the ground in 114_walker_character (close and far, each cascade) and metaballs touching the floor in 112_metaballs_deferred; no new acne on 119_ocean_fft's shoreline/islands if it receives shadows. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the shadow pass and its bias are Rae (lib/ShadowMaps.rae, Shadow3d.rae) and WGSL; no C. DONE (0.2.25): deferred casters cull back faces (bias 1, slope 1.5; the forward renderer keeps front-face 2/2.5 as a per-cache ShadowCasterBias), lighting drops the 1.5-texel normal offset for a receiver-plane bias from the G-buffer normal (dpdx/dpdy not usable: non-uniform control flow), 4-tap hardware-compare PCF instead of 8+12 taps. Contacts attach (112 metaball, 114 boxes/rocks/tree tiers), no new acne at noon or a 7:00 sun; frame 114 4.2->4.0, 112 7.4->7.0 ms. Not done: the optional one-tap TAA variant; the A2 widening penumbra is gone (docs/shadow-system-design.md).
- [x] #99243487 [render-perf-8: half-resolution bloom] {difficulty:4} The deferred renderer has no bloom. Add one: a downsample chain on the HDR lit target starting at half resolution in RG11B10Ufloat (dropping the smallest few mips), the first downsample with a soft-knee threshold (13 or 9 taps to avoid fireflies), a tent-filter upsample added back mip by mip, and the result added in the composite before tone mapping. App-set threshold (default ~0.3 in scene units), knee, intensity and on/off; off costs nothing. Verify with headless screenshots (bright sky/sun edges, emissive marks) and measure its GPU time at a phone's scene resolution. EXAMPLES: show it on 112_metaballs_deferred (shiny PBR metaballs), 119_ocean_fft (sun glint and whitecaps) and 114_walker_character (sky near the sun); off by default where an example looks worse; GPU time at a phone's scene resolution. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here bloom is new Rae lib + WGSL passes in the render graph; no new runtime C. DONE (0.2.26): lib/GbufferBloom.rae + bloom_*.wgsl, graph pass 'bloom' (antialias/taa -> bloom -> composite), setBloom / setBloomEnabled / RAE_BLOOM=0|1, off allocates and runs nothing. Default threshold 1.0 (not 0.3: at exposure 1.0 that is mid-grey and the whole 112 frame glowed), knee 0.5, intensity 0.2. On in 112 (highlights glow); off in 114 (sky haze over everything when facing the sun) and 119 (its glints are barely above white, bloom adds ~nothing). Cost at 1600x720 on the Mac: frame 2.3->2.8 ms (~0.42 ms GPU).

- [x] #95822908 {difficulty:4} [flaky test — 1038_server_time 'best of 15 waits within 0.5 ms' fails under suite load] Seen 2026-10-14 in the full suite during render-perf-9 (load average ~12 while the parallel runner was busy): the line printed false; the case passes when run alone (twice). The check measures timer wake-up precision, which a loaded machine cannot promise. Make it robust (e.g. a looser bound, more attempts, or a retry when the machine is busy) without hiding a real regression in the wait code, and say in the fixture what it can and cannot prove. DONE (0.2.28): the flake hid a real problem — macOS's sleep slack grew to ~25-50% of a sleep, so waitUntil's 7/8 first step overshot (4.4 ms median). waitUntil now sleeps half of what is left (0.05 ms median idle, 0.15 ms with 12 busy processes). Fixture 1038 checks the MEDIAN of 15 waits under 1 ms (plain sleep 5-8 ms, old code 1.5-4.4 ms: fails 2/2 when the old step is put back) instead of the best one, and says it cannot bound a single wait. Passes idle 2/2, loaded 3/3, full suite.
- [x] #11573123 [render-perf-10: TAA without ghosting] {difficulty:4} The TAA (deferred_taa.wgsl) keeps 90% history everywhere, with a 3x3 min/max clamp and nearest history fetches and no motion weighting, so thin moving things (wind-blown grass) ghost. Improve it: resolve in YCoCg space on Reinhard-compressed colour, clamp history to the 3x3 neighbourhood (variance clipping), bilinear (or Catmull-Rom) history fetch, a current-frame weight of about 0.05-0.2 rising with motion length, and a per-pixel reactive mask the G-buffer writes (grass and other alpha-tested/wind-animated geometry) that forces a high current weight there. Verify with a headless frame sequence of moving grass and a turning camera (ghost trails gone) against today's TAA. EXAMPLES: ghosting checks on 114_walker_character (wind-blown grass, a running character), 112_metaballs_deferred (moving metaballs) and 119_ocean_fft (moving water: mark it reactive too, or it smears). RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here the TAA changes and the reactive mask are Rae lib + WGSL (deferred_taa.wgsl, the G-buffer); no C. DONE (0.2.29): deferred_taa.wgsl resolves in YCoCg on Reinhard-compressed colour, variance-clips history (1 sigma), Catmull-Rom history fetch, current weight 0.05->0.2 with motion, reactive >= 0.6 (G-buffer gba.z = 1 from grass and metaballs; water found via the fresh opaque snapshot). RAE_TAA=0/1 override. 120 frames with a turning camera, ghost pixels vs no-TAA: 114 0.71->0.44%, 112 0.34->0.21%, 119 10.0->3.3%; 112's stepped double edge gone.
- [x] #43150958 [render-perf-11: late drawable] {difficulty:4} Acquire the surface texture as late as possible in a frame: record every offscreen pass (shadows, G-buffer, lighting, post) first and get the drawable only for the final composite/UI pass, so the CPU never blocks on a drawable while it still has other work to record. Check where wgpuSurfaceGetCurrentTexture is called today (runtime_gpu2d_*.c) and move it; measure the CPU time spent waiting for it before/after. EXAMPLES: measure the CPU wait for the drawable on 114_walker_character, 112_metaballs_deferred, 111_metaballs_forward and 119_ocean_fft. RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here wgpuSurfaceGetCurrentTexture is called inside C's present (runtime_gpu2d_frame.c:195; also runtime_gpu3d.c:715 for the forward path): make acquiring the drawable a one-call shim that Rae calls when its last pass begins, so WHEN to acquire is Rae's decision. DONE (0.2.30): the acquire was already the last step (inside C's present, after every pass was submitted); it is now Rae's: lib/gpu/Surface.rae calls rae_surface_acquire (one wgpuSurfaceGetCurrentTexture, keeps the hidden-window rule + recovery for 11b) and rae_surface_present at frame end, for the 2D/deferred and the forward path; C presents only screenshot. RAE_FRAME_TIMING prints the acquire wait: windowed 60 fps, 14-15 ms of 16.7 on 111/112/114/119 (CPU 1.4-2.5 ms), same split as HEAD. Found 121's reference mismatch (already on HEAD), queued.
- [x] #84371158 {difficulty:4} [bug — 121_ui_editor gate screenshots ~0.5-1.3% off the reference] Found 2026-10-14 while running gates for render-perf-11: every screenshot step of the 121 gate (the main shot, the component-list scroll, the save path, ...) reports MISMATCH, 0.49-1.26% of pixels more than 8 levels off (limits 0.1-0.3%). - [x] #29433410 {difficulty:4} [render-perf-11b: composite and UI straight into the drawable] Depends on render-perf-11 (late drawable). render-perf-5 kept the full-screen copy of the 2D canvas's target into the drawable (rae_g2d_present, ~0.15-0.18 ms at 2560x1600 on the Mac): the canvas opens its render pass over its own target at the START of the frame and the deferred composite and the UI draw into it, so rendering into the drawable would hold it through every 3D pass. Once the drawable is acquired late, open the canvas's pass (or the composite's) on the drawable's view and drop the copy, keeping: the no-acquire rule while the window is hidden (the macOS drawable leak, docs/runtime-c-audit.md row 16), a fallback to the offscreen target when no drawable is vended (headless, occluded), and the RAE_GPU2D_SCREENSHOT readback. Measure the frame on the iPhone 15 Pro too (a 2556x1179 copy is ~24 MB of traffic per frame). DONE (0.2.31): the canvas picks its frame target at the first pass that writes it (composite / tonemap / 2D beginFrame) and renders straight into the acquired drawable; present only presents. Offscreen + copy stay for headless, hidden window, no drawable, size mismatch, a load-first frame, RAE_GPU2D_SCREENSHOT and RAE_DIRECT_PRESENT=0. The present policy is Rae (lib/gpu/Surface.rae over the WebGPU bindings, lib/gpu/SurfaceRecovery.rae + fixture 1128, C test removed); C keeps 4 one-call shims; audit row 16 closed. Mac windowed: present 0.1-0.3 -> 0 ms, frames correct (screen captures of 112/106), RSS flat; headless screenshots unchanged. iPhone not measured (phone locked): queued as 'render-perf-11c: measure direct present on the iPhone 15 Pro'.

Keep f32 for depth, positions and reconstruction from depth. Request the feature at device creation when available, and keep an f32 path (the same WGSL with a type alias) for devices without it. Verify the images match (headless screenshots, a small tolerance) and measure the GPU time. EXAMPLES: image match and GPU time on 114_walker_character, 112_metaballs_deferred and 119_ocean_fft (the FFT and water shaders keep f32 where precision shows: displacement, normals at distance). RUNTIME C RULE (AGENTS.md 'Runtime C rule'): write the logic in Rae (lib/, shaders in WGSL); new runtime C only as one-call platform shims holding no policy (no loops, bookkeeping or choices); where this task touches existing C that holds policy, MOVE that policy to Rae instead of extending it, and record what moved in docs/runtime-c-audit.md. Here which device features to request is decided in C (runtime_webgpu.c, requiredFeatures around line 117): Rae chooses the features (shader-f16 when the adapter has it) and passes them in; the shader variants (f16 / f32 alias) are chosen in Rae. DONE (0.2.34), in part: Rae chooses the device features (lib/gpu/DeviceFeatures.rae: rg11b10, timestamps, shader-f16 when the adapter has them; audit row 17), shaders compose a precision prelude (lib/precision_f16.wgsl / precision_f32.wgsl, `half` alias) picked by deviceHasShaderF16(), and deferred_light.wgsl shades in half (depth, reconstruction, shadows, AO weights, sky and the GGX lobe stay f32). Images match (119: 1.9% of pixels off by <= 14 levels). Mac GPU time: no measurable change. Composite / SSAO / bloom / AA NOT converted (texture-bound, no gain expected); the phone was locked: split to 'render-perf-12b: measure f16 lighting on the iPhone, then decide on the other passes'.

- [x] #44566384 {difficulty:4} [forward-rae-6: SSAO in the forward pass] SSAO reads a depth and a normal per pixel (lib/GbufferAo.rae binds gbufferTargetDepth and gbufferTargetA), and the forward pass keeps neither: its 4x depth is memoryless and WebGPU cannot resolve depth. Add SSAO to the forward renderer as an OPTION (off by default, so 111 and others pay nothing): a 1x depth + normal pre-pass at the scene size (depth stored, octahedral normal in one rgb10a2 target, the same draws with a cheap depth/normal material), then the existing half-resolution SSAO over it, then the 4x forward pass reading the AO texture in forward_entry.wgsl / forward_static.wgsl / forward_sdf.wgsl (ssao from the AO texture at the pixel instead of 1, the same upsample as deferred_light.wgsl). Graph: shadow -> prepass -> ssao -> forward -> bloom -> composite. Enable it in examples/126_walker_forward (forward-rae-5). Measure on the iPhone 15 Pro (RAE_IOS_HIGH_REFRESH=1 build, RAE_DRS=off, same thermal state): 126 with SSAO, 126 without, and 114 deferred. docs/forward-msaa-study.md measured a depth pre-pass 6-89% slower on Apple GPUs; if forward + pre-pass + SSAO ends up slower than 114's deferred frame, record the numbers and close with [question] asking whether to keep it, rather than shipping a slower path. Fixture 1130 gains an SSAO case (the lit image darker in a corner than without SSAO, same elsewhere). RESULT 2026-10-15 (0.2.42): implemented and committed, OFF by default. createForwardRendererWithSsao: the G-buffer pass is the 1x pre-pass (only depth + normals stored), the deferred SSAO runs over it, the forward shaders read the AO (override forwardSsao, compiled out when off); fixture 1130 checks it darkens like the deferred SSAO; 126 has it opt-in (RAE_FORWARD_SSAO=1, in its gate). iPhone 15 Pro, same thermal state: 120 Hz build 126+SSAO 19.6/20.2 ms vs 114 16.0/16.0 ms vs 126 without 11.0/11.4 ms; 60 Hz build 126+SSAO ~49 fps while 114 and 126 hold 60. A pre-pass without grass was no faster. Numbers in docs/forward-msaa-study.md section 8. QUESTION: forward + pre-pass + SSAO is about 4 ms slower than deferred for the walker. Keep the option as it is (opt-in, off), invest in a cheaper pre-pass (a depth + normal-only material per G-buffer material, plus finding where the 9 ms go; may still not beat deferred), or remove the forward SSAO option again? ANSWERED 2026-10-15 by the user: 126 is simply the forward example with SSAO, so SSAO is always on in 126 (RAE_FORWARD_SSAO removed); the speed against 114 is recorded, not a reason to turn it off.
- [x] #31175069 [codegen: List instances for nested collection literals] {difficulty:3} A list literal nested inside a struct literal that is itself an argument does not get its List(T) functions generated: e.g. `addPropKind(field: f, kind: { lods: { { parts: { part(id: 1) }, maxDistance: 30.0, castsShadow: true, facesCamera: false } }, radius: 2.0, centerHeight: 2.0 })` (lib/app3d/PropField types) passes sema, then clang fails with "call to undeclared function rae_core_List_createList_rae_PropLod_..." / "...List_add_rae_PropPart_..." when no other code in the program names List(PropLod) / List(PropPart). Building the lists step by step with List.create + add works (compiler case 1136_prop_field does that, with a note). Fix: collect the element types of nested collection literals (in struct-literal fields, in call arguments) for List instantiation; add a compiler case with the literal form and remove the note from 1136. — DONE: c_discovery.c now (1) recurses into a call's arguments with each parameter's declared type as the expected type (for a resolved non-generic callee), so an untyped struct/list literal argument knows what it is, and (2) propagates the element type into a struct-literal element of a collection literal, so lists inside that struct are discovered. Both together instantiate the nested List(T) createList/add/drop. 1136 now uses the literal form (note removed); new case 1139 covers the struct-arg and bare-list-arg forms with a leak check. Full suite 796 passed, 0 failed.
- [x] #86983155 [codegen: writes through a struct member passed to mod lost] {difficulty:4} In a downstream app, a builder function did `var field: PropField = createPropField(...)`, added kinds to it, moved it into a local struct (`var holder: Holder = { propField: own field, ... }`), then in two nested `loop var` loops with `continue`s and a `match pick.kind { case ... { place(field: holder.propField, kind: ..., x: ..., ...) ; counter = counter + 1 } }` called a function taking `field: mod PropField` that calls addPropPlacement (a List(PropPlacement).add). The counters went up (3,610), but none of those placements were in holder.propField afterwards (it held only 24, added by another function that took `holder: mod Holder` and called the same place(field: holder.propField, ...)). finishPropField(field: holder.propField) then exited the process with code 1 and no message, at different points on different runs. Keeping the PropField a plain local until it was finished, and moving it into the struct at the end, fixed both. A small program with the same shape (a struct with a List member moved in with own, put(field: holder.field) in a match arm in a loop) did NOT reproduce it, so the trigger is something more (PropField is large: Lists of structs holding Lists, List(Mat4), List(InstanceBatch); the call had seven arguments). Narrow it down from lib/app3d/PropField with a struct holding a PropField, fix, and add a compiler case. Related note in lib/RendererDeferred: writing a nested struct field through a mod parameter does not reliably land. If this is the last task in the QUEUE.md to be done, push to git at the end. if not, add this note to push to git at the last added task. — DONE as far as it can be: NOT REPRODUCIBLE, so no compiler change. The failing builder was recovered from the downstream session's transcript (the holder built right after the kinds, place(field: holder.propField, kind: holder.treeKinds.copyAtFallback(...), ...) in match arms in two nested loops with continues, a `holder: mod Holder` roof function; the session also had a `let field: mod PropField => holder.propField` alias version). Run inside the downstream app exactly at its commit, with the Rae compiler and lib of that hour (worktree at 31f74967, same compiler sources as fb4c295d) and with today's: all 6,713 placements land, finishPropField finishes, exit 0. A plain list member, a mod alias and two mod members in one call were also tried: all land. The shared checkout's compiler was rebuilt by another session minutes before the failing runs, which may explain a transient wrong binary, but that is not proven. Added compiler case 1140_prop_field_in_holder (the reported shapes with the real PropField: own move into a holder, nested loops with continue, match arms with the seven-argument place, a `mod Holder` function, a `=>` mod alias, then finishPropField: 1,654 of 1,654 land). The RendererDeferred/134 'nested field through mod does not reliably land' note is already gone from the tree; the remaining notes say it is reliable (fixture 707, passes).

I believe it is something like 1 minute per day now. Make it 30 minute days. - In the ... menu add a menuitem to skip 6(?) hours (is that a good value of hours to skip), so can skip the night. — DONE: new lib/Moon.rae: the moon's place and phase from the year, day, local solar time, latitude and longitude (the Astronomical Almanac's low-precision lunar terms, placed relative to lib/Sky's sun so the two agree), moonLightLevel (the lit fraction^1.5, faded at the horizon: a full moon gives far more light than a crescent) and moonPhaseName; case 1161 checks the phases of January 2024 (new 11th, first quarter 18th, full 25th, last quarter 2 Feb) and that the full moon stands opposite the sun. The sky: Sky has the moon (direction, disc, night light, night ambient); the shader draws the disc lit on the side facing the sun (so the phase is the geometry), a dark blue night sky that brightens and glows round the moon with its light, and the sky models now use the sky's own sun (LightU gained skySun, moon, night). The light: settingsKeyLightDir/Color make the scene's one light the moon at night (moon shadows; a full moon is about 0.45 of the moon's colour, a moonless night 0.03), settingsMoonLight is the gameplay value (how light the night is). Toggle: the settings panel's Moon button and the walker's '...' menu item ('Moon: waxing gibbous, 78% lit' / 'Moon: off'); off, the night is exactly what it was. The settings gained a longitude slider. Walker days last 30 minutes (TimeOfDay.daySeconds, default still 1 minute elsewhere); the '...' menu has 'Skip 6 hours' (a quarter day: 21:00 -> 03:00 past the night), case 1162. RAE_WALKER_DAY sets the start day for screenshots (193 in 1995 is a full moon). 134/135/EditorPanel use the key light too. Checked: full suite 819 passed, 0 failed; gates 134, 135, 136, 137, 143 pass; screenshots of a crescent and a full-moon night (136 and 137) and the menu. Found and queued: Math.clamp is uncallable (`val:` does not parse) and a const-valued field default does not compile. VERSION 0.2.88.
- [x] #59023164 [parser: `val` cannot be an argument name, so Math.clamp is uncallable] lib/Math's `func clamp(val: view Float, low:, high:)` (and the Int overload) declares a parameter named `val`, but a call `Math.clamp(val: x, low: 0.0, high: 1.0)` fails to parse ("expected argument name (subsequent arguments must be named)" at `val:`), so nothing in the tree calls it (found while writing lib/Moon, which uses Math.min/Math.max instead). Either `val` is a reserved word the declaration should have been refused for (then rename the parameter to `value` and reject `val` as a parameter name), or the call-argument parser mis-handles it; add a compiler case calling Math.clamp. — DONE: `val` is meant to be a name (parser.c is_ident_like accepts it, like `copy`), but both call-argument parsers (calls and method calls) recognised a named argument only for TOK_IDENT before `:`, so `val:` was read as a positional value and the call failed. They now use is_ident_like, so `val:` and `copy:` work as argument names. New case 1163_named_arg_val (Math.clamp for Float and Int, first argument and after `1.0 -`, a function and a method with a `val` parameter). lib/Moon and SettingsDialog use Math.clamp again. VERSION 0.2.89. Checked: full suite 820 passed, 0 failed. Queued: `copy` can be a parameter name but cannot be read in a body.

Either accept `copy` there too (check it cannot clash with a `copy x` expression form) or refuse `copy` as a parameter name; add a compiler case. — DONE: no expression starts with `copy` (the parser matches TOK_KW_COPY only as a type's parameter mode), so the primary-expression parser now takes it as a name beside `val` (parser.c). New case 1164_param_named_copy: a parameter named `copy` read in the body (`val * copy`, `copy + copy`), named in calls, and a type's own copy function still called as `box.copy()`. - [x] #16483925 [compiler bug: `copy` and `own` parameters are read-only in the function body] `func f(a: copy Particle) { a.x = 7 }` fails with "cannot mutate through read-only view 'a'", and `a = { x: 8, name: "c" }` with "cannot assign to read-only view identifier 'a'"; `own` is the same (probed 2026-10-20 in /tmp/bindprobe r7_copy / r7_own). A `copy` or `own` parameter is the callee's own value, like a `var`: its fields and the whole value must be writable, and writing it never reaches the caller (the caller's value stays as it was for `copy`; for `own` the caller's name has ended). Find where sema marks these parameters read-only (the view check is applied to them), fix it, and add a case: write a field and the whole value of a `copy` and an `own` parameter, and check the caller's `let` value is unchanged after the `copy` call. Only `view` parameters stay read-only. — DONE: sema.c (function parameters into the symbol table) took every struct parameter that was not `mod` or `view` as a view parameter (read-only), which caught `copy` and `own` too. Now only `view` is read-only (a bare struct parameter, already an error, stays read-only so it gives one error, not two). The generated C already gave the callee its own deep copy, so writes never reach the caller. New case 1166_copy_own_params_writable: fields (including a List field) and the whole value of a `copy` and an `own` parameter written; the caller's `let` unchanged after the copy calls; no leaks at exit. VERSION 0.2.91. Checked: full suite 828 passed, 0 failed.
- [x] #34874765 [codegen bug: a struct named `P` breaks `List(P)`] With `type P { x: Int, name: String }`, any `List(P)` (a local, a `ret List(P)`, `List.create(P, cap: 2)`, a `List(P) { {...} }` literal) makes C that does not compile: `unknown type name 'rae_List_rae_P'` / `use of undeclared identifier 'rae_List_rae_P'`; renaming the type `Particle` makes the same program work (probed 2026-10-20). Probably a one-letter type name is taken for a generic type parameter somewhere in the List specialisation registration, so `List(P)` is never declared. Find it (single-letter type names are bad style, but a type's name must never be mistaken for a type parameter), fix it, and add a case with a one-letter struct name used as List(P) through a literal, List.create and a function's ret. — DONE: c_backend.c is_concrete_type took any one-capital-letter type name for an unresolved generic parameter, so register_generic_type / register_function_specialization skipped `List(P)` and its C type was never declared. It now takes the compiler context and calls a one-letter name a parameter only when no type or enum of that name is declared (decl_index_find_type / decl_index_find_enum). New case 1167_one_letter_type_name: a struct `P` through a List(P) literal, List.create, a function's ret and a generic call with T: P, and an enum `E` in a List(E); a generic function's own T still a parameter. No leaks. VERSION 0.2.92. Checked: full suite 829 passed, 0 failed.
- [x] #88140274 [binding `=` 1/6: the semantics doc] Decision 2026-10-20: `=` is the only binder; `=>` goes. A binding's mode on the same line decides what the name denotes: no mode = a fresh deep copy of the right-hand side; `view T` / `mod T` = an alias of the place on the right; `own x` on the right = a move. Replace (not patch) the old README argument ('`=` always copies, `=>` always aliases, so the operator carries the meaning') with: '`=` initializes a binding of the declared type; `view T` and `mod T` are types whose values are aliases, exactly as they already are for parameters and `loop let`. Aliasing is marked by the mode keyword everywhere, and nowhere else.' State the one subtlety: `=` on a declaration line initializes; `=` on any other line writes into the place the name denotes; aliases can never be rebound (so `let m: mod Particle = world.a` then `m = world.b` deep-copies world.b INTO world.a, it is not a rebind), and a `view` on the left of a non-declaration `=` is an error. Write the approved table into docs/binding-modes-design.md (the 2026-09-30 option N section) and the README binding section, rows: (1) `let x: T = v`: not rebindable, no `x = v`, no `x.f = v`; (2) `var x: T = v`: `x = v` replaces x with a deep copy, fields writable, the source never changes; (3) `let x: view T = place`: no rebind, no write, no field write (errors 'cannot assign through read-only view' / 'cannot mutate through read-only view'); (4) `let x: mod T = place`: no rebind, `x = v` deep-copies v into the place, fields writable; (5) `var x: view T` / `var x: mod T`: errors ('aliases are bind-once; declare with let'); (6) `loop let x: view T in list` read-only, `loop let x: mod T in list` writes the element (`x = v` deep-copies into it), `loop var x: mod T` an error, `loop var x: T` a writable local copy; (7) parameters: bare `a: T` an error, `view` read-only, `mod` writes the caller's place (`a = v` deep-copies into it) and a `let` cannot be passed to it. Errors in every alias row: a view/mod of a temporary (`let v: view T = makeThing()`: 'cannot bind a reference to a call that returns ownership'), a `view` passed on to `mod`, and (from step 2) a `mod` alias or `mod` loop over a `let`. Record that `var x: mod T` was considered and rejected: `var` means rebindable and aliases never are. Docs only, one commit. — DONE: docs/binding-modes-design.md §1 now opens with the 2026-10-20 decision (the three meanings, the step-1 sentence, the one subtlety with the `m = world.b` write-through example, the approved 7-row table with its error messages, the errors shared by every alias row, and `var x: mod T` recorded as rejected); its examples, the matrix header and §3's refactoring bullet use `=`, and the status line says the compiler keeps `=>` until the parser step. README.md: the '`=` copies, `=>` binds' bullet and the 'operator carries the meaning' argument are replaced by the new sentence, a 'Bindings, one table' section holds the table, and its code examples use `=` (they compile once step 3 lands; examples/09_playlist, which the first one mirrors, is swept in step 4). Docs only; no code or tests changed.

Passing a `let` to a `mod` parameter is already refused ('cannot pass 'p' to 'mod' parameter ...: 'p' ... is a 'let', which is frozen'); give (a) and (b) the same error, naming the `let` and its line. Fix the tree if the error finds writes into `let`s (make those bindings `var`). - [x] #94632378 {difficulty:4} Compiler bug: a bare name must follow the same module rule for functions as it already does for consts. Rae's rule: a bare name resolves in the file's own module and the modules it OPENS; when more than one of them gives it, the use is ambiguous and must be qualified. The compiler enforces this for consts (case 966: two opened modules, 'matGrass' is ambiguous) and for project folders (case 677: 'tick' in two folders, qualify it), but NOT for functions: (1) a project function with the same name and parameters as one in an opened lib module wins silently (probe: `open app3d/UiModes` plus a project `func isShortWindowHeight(height: view Float) ret Bool { ret false }`; the bare call returns the project's false, no diagnostic); (2) two opened lib modules with the same function (`open GltfSkin` + `open ShadowMaps`, both `floatAt(list:, index:)`) compile and one is picked silently; (3) the same holds for a project const against an opened lib const. Make all three the ambiguity error consts already give, naming both modules and the fix (qualify it, or open only one). Overloads inside ONE module (same name, other labels) stay legal. Keep what works: with the lib module only imported, the bare name is the project's and `Module.name` the lib's, and a lib's own code keeps its own names (case 1168). New cases for (1)-(3). Then build every example and run the suite; fix lib code the error finds (lib/GltfSkin and lib/ShadowMaps both define floatAt; a downstream game had dozens of such copies hidden this way). VISUAL TESTS OK. — DONE (compiler 0.2.98): resolve_function_overload now reports a bare call as ambiguous when two modules visible bare here (opened, the project as one module, the prelude) declare a function with the same name, labels and parameter types (each compared as its own module resolved it; generics by their written signature; externs skipped, they are one C function); a function declared in the calling file wins. sema_resolve_bare_global no longer lets an opened lib const win over a project const in another file or a prelude const: two visible modules are the error, while an explicit `open` still picks one of two PROJECT modules (1016). Message for both: ''X' is ambiguous: it is declared in both 'A' and 'B', which are both open here; open only one of them, or qualify the call (`B.x(...)`)'. Overloads by other labels or other parameter types stay legal (drop(this: mod Event) beside drop(this: mod Queue) was the first false hit and is not reported). New cases 1184 (project vs opened lib function), 1185 (open Float4 + open Float8: lowering()), 1186 (project vs opened lib const), 1187 (a file's own const wins). 965 pinned the old pick (an opened lib const over the project's painter/Own const): painter moved out to 1187. Lib fixes the error found: floatAt removed from lib/GltfSkin and lib/ShadowMaps (both were list.copyAtFallback(index:, fallback: 0.0); 101 calls in 6 files, case 577 too); lib/ui/WindowGeometry calls Filesystem.makeDir (creates parents; Sys.makeDir in the prelude makes one level); physics: Scalar's pi removed (Math's, the same f32), MeshQuery's copy of VectorMath.modifiedCross removed, Mesh's leafNode (3) renamed meshLeafNode — DynamicTree's leafNode (2147483648) is visible in the same package and DynamicTreeRebuild had picked by its open; 121 dropped its copy of UiShell.uiActionFired. Checked: emit-c of all 106 programs (only the 5 stress programs that fail on purpose), full suite 847 passed 0 failed, example gates 122, 124, 136, 137, 143 (5 passed; 122 equal to its golden) and 121 passed, example-copy gate passed.

- [x] #14407207 {difficulty:4} Compiler bug: type and enum names must belong to their module, like every other name. Today they are program-wide: two modules that both declare `type Point` cannot be in one program at all (sema.c sema_report_duplicate_type, case 989 'type names are program-wide'), even when the program only IMPORTS one of them (probe: `import Device` plus a project `type Pointer { x: Int }` is refused because lib/Device has `enum Pointer`). So a lib adding a type can break any program that has one of the same name, though nothing there opens it. Rae's module rule already says how it should work: a type is reached bare in its own module and the modules the file opens, and as `Module.Type` through import; two opened modules with the same type name make a bare use ambiguous at the USE, not an error at the declaration. Make the compiler do that: type identity is module plus name in the type registry, C names carry the module (so two `Point` structs both emit), generics, component tables, reflection and the JSON/state formats keep working (a type's written name stays its own name). Case 989 changes to two `Point` types living side by side, each module using its own, plus cases for `Module.Type` through import, the ambiguity at a bare use with both opened, and a lib type next to a same-named project type under import. Large: split if needed (registry and C names first, then the use-site rules). Build every example and run the suite. VISUAL TESTS OK. — DONE (compiler 0.2.101), in one step (no split needed): the type registry already keyed a struct by its declaration, so the work was the NAMES. New src/type_names.c runs at the start of sema over the merged program: for a type/enum name declared in two files or more, every declaration but one gets an internal name carrying its module (`Point__in_points_Right`; a lib declaration keeps the written name, so a type the compiler knows by name keeps it; an internal name cannot be written, a type name has no `_`), and every use — type refs, literals, casts, generic args, `X.case`, `X.create`, match patterns — is pointed at the declaration its file sees: its own file's, else the one visible bare (an explicit open picks between two project modules), else the one it imports. Two visible bare is an error at the USE: 'type 'Pointer' is ambiguous: it is declared in both 'Device' and 'input/Pointers', which are both open here; open only one of them'. The same pass resolves the new `Module.Type` (parser: a qualifier on a type ref; formatter prints it) in types and expressions (`Device.Pointer.fine`), through import/open/`as`, and errors on a type the module does not have. type_written_name gives the written name back for string conversion, typeName (fields loops, generic params) and so JSON/component names. One module declaring a type twice is still an error ('declared twice in one module'). Cases: 989 is now 989_same_type_name_two_modules (two Points side by side: literals, List(Point), toJson/fromJson), 1189 (import Device + a project Pointer: bare is the project's, Device.Pointer the lib's), 1190 (ambiguous bare use with both open, and an unknown Device.Nothing), 1191 (declared twice in one module). Also checked by hand: two modules' generic Box(T) and enum Kind (match, is, toString), ComponentTable(Point) + typeName, no leaks. Checked: emit-c of all 106 programs; the C of 121, 136 and 106 is byte-identical to the previous compiler's except the checkout path inside embedded shader paths (so no visual run was needed: nothing in the tree clashes); full suite 851 passed, 0 failed; docs/module-namespacing.md's 'Type names are program-wide' section rewritten.
